\displaystyle\text{Pr}\left[\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{1},l-1}\rvert<Q_{l-1},g_{l}(i_{1})=1\mid i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},E_{2,d},\textsc{goodest}(i_{1})\right]

\displaystyle=\text{Pr}\left[\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{1},l-1}\rvert<Q_{l-1}\mid i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},E_{2,d},\textsc{goodest}(i_{1})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot\text{Pr}\left[g_{l}(i_{1})=1\mid i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},E_{2,d},\textsc{goodest}(i_{1})\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(14)

The second product probability term simplifies to
(1/2)\pm\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))
. Substituting in Eqn. (
A.1
), we have,

\displaystyle\text{Pr}\left[\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{1},l-1}\rvert<Q_{l-1},g_{l}(i_{1})=1\mid i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},E_{2,d},\textsc{goodest}(i_{1})\right]

\displaystyle=\text{Pr}\left[\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{1},l-1}\rvert<Q_{l-1}\mid i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},E_{2,d},\textsc{goodest}(i_{1})\right]\left(1/2\pm\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(15)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\text{Pr}\left[\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{1},l-1}\rvert<Q_{l-1},g_{l}(i_{1})=1\mid i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},E_{2,d},\textsc{goodest}(i_{1}),\mathcal{G}\right]

\displaystyle=\text{Pr}\left[\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{1},l-1}\rvert<Q_{l-1},g_{l}(i_{1})=1\mid i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},E_{2,d},\textsc{goodest}(i_{1})\right]\pm\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))

\displaystyle=\text{Pr}\left[\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{1},l-1}\rvert<Q_{l-1}\mid i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},E_{2,d},\textsc{goodest}(i_{1})\right]\left(1/2\pm\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\pm\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))\kern 5.0pt.

(16)

Substituting in Eqn. (
A.1
), we have,

Term3

\displaystyle=2\text{Pr}\left[\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{1},l-1}\rvert<Q_{l-1},g_{l}(i_{1})=1\mid i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},E_{2,d},\mathcal{G},\textsc{goodest}(i_{1})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot\left(1\pm 2^{l-1}\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))\right)

\displaystyle=2\text{Pr}\left[\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{1},l-1}\rvert<Q_{l-1}\mid i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},E_{2,d},\textsc{goodest}(i_{1})\right]\left(1/2\pm\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\pm\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))

\displaystyle=\text{Pr}\left[\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{1},l-1}\rvert<Q_{l-1}\mid i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},E_{2,d},\textsc{goodest}(i_{1})\right]\left(1\pm\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\pm\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))\kern 5.0pt.

(17)

Adding Eqns. (
A.1
) and (
A.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\text{ Term 1 }+\text{ Term 2}+\text{ Term 3}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm 2^{l-1}\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))\right)\pm\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))

\displaystyle=1\pm 2^{l}\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))\kern 5.0pt.

Corollary 26

Let
i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}\in[n]
and
S\subset\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}\}
containing
i_{1}
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}\textsf{~consist. with~}~i_{1}}2^{l_{1}}\text{Pr}\left[i_{1}\in\bar{G}_{l_{1}}\left|\bigwedge_{j=h+1}^{d}i_{j}\in\mathcal{S}_{l_{j}},\mathcal{G},\bigwedge_{j\in S}\textsc{goodest}(j)\right.\right]

\displaystyle=1\pm 2^{\mathsf{level}(i_{1})}\lvert S\rvert\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))\kern 5.0pt.

Proof:
The proof proceeds identically as the proof for Lemma
25
.

Lemma 27

[Re-statement of Lemma
7
.]
Let
i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}\in[n]
and distinct
1\leq h\leq d
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\textsf{~consist. with~}i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h]\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+l_{2}+\ldots+l_{h}}\text{Pr}\left[\left.\bigwedge_{j=1}^{h}i_{j}\in\bar{G}_{l_{j}}\right|\bigwedge_{j=h+1}^{d}i_{j}\in\mathcal{S}_{l_{j}},\mathcal{G},\bigwedge_{j=1}^{d}\textsc{goodest}(i_{j})\right]

\displaystyle\in\prod_{j=1}^{h}\left(1\pm 2^{\mathsf{level}(i_{j})+1}\cdot\left(\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},\delta)+dn^{-\Omega(1)}\right)\right)

Proof:

The proof proceeds by induction on
h
.

The base case occurs when
h=1
and has been proved in Lemma
25
and Corollary
26
.

Induction Case.
Let
E_{h+1,d}
denote the event
\bigwedge_{j=h+1}^{d}i_{j}\in\mathcal{S}_{l_{j}}
,
G_{h}
denote the event
\bigwedge_{j=1}^{h}i_{j}\in\bar{G}_{l_{j}}
and
K_{d}=\bigwedge_{j=1}^{d}\textsc{goodest}(i_{j})
. Also, for simplicity, let
\delta^{\prime}
denote
\min(n^{-\Omega(1)},O(\delta))
. In this notation, the statement of the lemma can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\text{ consistent with }i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h]\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+\ldots+l_{h+1}}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\mid E_{h+1,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]\in\prod_{j=1}^{h}(1\pm 2^{\mathsf{level}(i_{j})+1})\left(\delta^{\prime}+dn^{-\Omega(1)}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(18)

We assume that for a fixed
h\in[d-1]
, the induction hypothesis holds, that is,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\text{ consistent with }i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h]\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+\ldots+l_{h}}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}|E_{h+1,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]\in\prod_{j=1}^{h+1}(1\pm 2^{\mathsf{level}(i_{j})+1})\left(\delta^{\prime}+dn^{-\Omega(1)}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

We now have to show that the following equation holds.

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\text{ consistent with }i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h+1]\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+\ldots+l_{h+1}}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h+1}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]\in\prod_{j=1}^{h+1}(1\pm 2^{\mathsf{level}(i_{j})+1})\left(\delta^{\prime}+dn^{-\Omega(1)}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Induction Case 1:
Suppose there exists a
j\in[h+1]
such that
i_{j}\in\text{mid}(G_{l})
. Without loss of generality, let
j=h+1
and
i_{h+1}\in\text{mid}(G_{l})
(this can be done by rearranging the indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{h+1}
, without affecting the statement). Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\text{ consistent with }i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h+1]\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+\ldots l_{h+1}}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h+1}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\text{ consistent with }i_{j}\\
\forall j\in\{1,\ldots,h\}\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+\ldots l_{h}+l}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\wedge(i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l})|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\text{ consistent with }i_{j}\\
\forall j\in\{1,\ldots,h\}\end{subarray}}2^{l+l_{1}+\ldots l_{h}}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}|i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}\wedge E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]\text{Pr}\left[i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}\mid E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

(19)

The term

\displaystyle\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}|i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}\wedge E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}|E_{h+1,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]\prod_{j=1}^{h}(1\pm 2^{\mathsf{level}(i_{j})+1}\left(O(\delta)+dn^{-\Omega(1)}\right)

by the induction hypothesis.
The second term in Eqn. (
A.1
) is evaluated as follows. By
d
-wise independence,
\text{Pr}\left[i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}\mid E_{h+2,d}\right]=2^{-l}
. Also,
\mathcal{G}
holds with probability
1-\delta^{\prime}
and
K_{d}
holds with probability
1-dn^{-\Omega(1)}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{G}\wedge K_{d}
holds with probability
1-\delta^{\prime}-dn^{-\Omega(1)}
. Hence, by Lemma
23
,

\text{Pr}\left[i_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}\mid E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]\in 2^{-l}\pm O(\delta^{\prime}+dn^{-\Omega(1)})\kern 5.0pt.

Therefore, Eqn. (
A.1
) can be bounded as

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{h+1}(1\pm 2^{\mathsf{level}(i_{j})+1}\left(O(\delta)+dn^{-\Omega(1)}\right)

there by proving this case.

Induction Case 2:
Suppose there exists an index
j\in[h+1]
such that
i_{j}\in\text{lmargin}(G_{l})
. By reordering the indices, we can assume without loss of generality that
i_{h+1}\in\text{lmargin}(G_{l})
. Conditional on
\mathcal{G}
and
goodest
(i)
, as shown in Lemma
24
, the following holds.

1.

i_{h+1}\in\bar{G}_{l}
iff
i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}
and
\lvert\hat{x}_{h+1,l}\rvert\geq T_{l}
.

2.

i_{h+1}\in\bar{G}_{l+1}
iff
i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}
and
Q_{l}<\lvert\hat{x}_{h+1,l}\rvert<T_{l}
.

3.

i_{h+1}\in\bar{G}_{r}
, for any
r\in[L]\setminus\{l,l+1\}
.

Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\text{ consistent with }i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h+1]\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+\ldots l_{h+1}}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h+1}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\text{ consistent with }i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h]\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+\ldots+l_{h}+l}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\wedge(i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}\wedge\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l}\rvert\geq T_{l})|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\text{ consistent with }i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h]\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+\ldots+l_{h}+l+1}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\wedge(i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}\wedge(\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l}\rvert<T_{l},K_{i_{h+1}}=1)|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

(20)

Since the coin toss
K_{i_{h+1}}=1
occurs with probability 1/2 and is independent of all the other random bits, the second probability expression in the
RHS
of Eqn. (
A.1
), we have,

\displaystyle\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\wedge(i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}\wedge\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l}\rvert<T_{l},K_{i_{h+1}}=1)|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=(1/2)\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\wedge(i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}\wedge\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l}\rvert<T_{l})|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Thus, the
RHS
of Eqn. (
A.1
) is simplified as follows.

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\text{ consistent with }i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h]\end{subarray}}\left(2^{l_{1}+\ldots+l_{h}+l}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\wedge(i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}\wedge\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l}\rvert\geq T_{l})|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.~~+2^{l_{1}+\ldots+l_{h}+l+1}(1/2)\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\wedge(i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}\wedge\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l}\rvert<T_{l})|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\text{ consistent with }i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h]\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+\ldots+l_{h}+l}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\wedge i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\text{ consistent with }i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h]\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+\ldots+l_{h}+l}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}|i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}\wedge E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot\text{Pr}\left[i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

(21)

where the first step follows from probability axioms of union of exclusive events, and the last step follows from the definition of conditional probability.
Now, as discussed earlier in this proof,
\text{Pr}\left[i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}|E_{h+2,d}\right]=2^{-l}
and therefore,
\text{Pr}\left[i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]=2^{-l}\pm\min(O(\delta),n^{-\Omega(1)})+dn^{-\Omega(1)}
. Also, the event
i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l}\wedge E_{h+2,d}=E_{h+1,d}
. Hence, the probability expression in the
RHS
of Eqn. (
A.1
) is

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\text{ consistent with }i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h]\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+\ldots+l_{h}}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}|E_{h+1,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right](1\pm 2^{l}\min(O(\delta),n^{-\Omega(1)})+dn^{-\Omega(1)})

\displaystyle=(1\pm 2^{l}\min(O(\delta),n^{-\Omega(1)})+dn^{-\Omega(1)})\prod_{j=1}^{h}\left(1\pm 2^{l_{j}+1}\min(O(\delta),n^{-\Omega(1)})\right)

by the induction hypothesis, thereby proving this induction case.

Induction Case 3:
For this case, it suffices to assume that for each
j\in[h+1]
,
i_{j}\in\text{rmargin}(G_{l_{j}})
. Assume that we re-order the items so that
\lvert x_{i_{h+1}}\rvert\geq\lvert x_{i_{h}}\rvert\geq\ldots\geq\lvert x_{i_{1}}\rvert
. Let
\mathsf{level}(i_{h+1})=l
. Therefore,
the expression for the
LHS
is

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\textsf{~consist. with~}i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h+1]\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+l_{2}+\ldots+l_{h+1}}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h+1}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(22)

We are given that
i_{h+1}\in\text{rmargin}(G_{l})
. Conditional on
\mathcal{G}
and
goodest
, we have the following.

1.

i_{h+1}
may be discovered at level
l-1
(only if
i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1})
or possibly at level
l
(only if
i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l})
, but at no lower level than
l-1
.

2.

i_{h+1}\in\bar{G}_{l-1}
if
i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1}
and
\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l-1}\rvert\geq T_{l-1}
.

3.

i_{h+1}\in\bar{G}_{l}
in one of two mutually exclusive ways.

(a)

i\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1}
and
Q_{l-1}=T_{l-1}(1-\bar{\epsilon}))<\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l-1}\rvert<T_{l}
and the coin toss
K_{i}=1
.

(b)

i\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1}
and
g_{l}(i_{h+1})=1
and
\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l-1}\rvert<Q_{l-1}
.

The probability term in Eqn. (
22
) can be written as follows.

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{h+1}\textsf{~consist. with~}i_{h+1}}2^{l_{h+1}}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h+1}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=2^{l-1}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\wedge i_{h+1}\in\bar{G}_{l-1}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle~~+2^{l}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\wedge i_{h+1}\in\bar{G}_{l}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=2^{l-1}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},\lvert\hat{x}_{h+1,l-1}\rvert\geq T_{l-1}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle~~+2^{l}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},Q_{l-1}<\lvert\hat{x}_{h+1,l-1}\rvert<T_{l-1},K_{i}=1\mid E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle~~+2^{l}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},g_{l}(i_{h+1})=1,Q_{l-1}>\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l-1}\rvert\mid E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

(23)

We simplify the above equation to consider the following expression, which we will relate back to the
RHS
of Eqn. (
A.1
).

\displaystyle=2^{l-1}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},\lvert\hat{x}_{h+1,l-1}\rvert\geq T_{l-1}|E_{h+2,d},\right]

\displaystyle~~+2^{l}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},Q_{l-1}<\lvert\hat{x}_{h+1,l-1}\rvert<T_{l-1},K_{i}=1\mid E_{h+2,d}\right]

\displaystyle~~+2^{l}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},g_{l}(i_{h+1})=1,Q_{l-1}>\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l-1}\rvert\mid E_{h+2,d}\right]

\displaystyle=2^{l-1}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},\lvert\hat{x}_{h+1,l-1}\rvert\geq T_{l-1}|E_{h+2,d},\right]

\displaystyle~~+2^{l-1}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},Q_{l-1}<\lvert\hat{x}_{h+1,l-1}\rvert<T_{l-1}\mid E_{h+2,d}\right]

\displaystyle~~+2^{l-1}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},Q_{l-1}>\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l-1}\rvert\mid E_{h+2,d}\right]

(24)

The last step follows since
g_{l}(i_{h+1})
is independent of
g_{r}
for
r\neq l
and comes from a
d
-wise independent family.
So Eqn. (
A.1
) becomes

\displaystyle=2^{l-1}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\mid E_{h+2,d}\right]

(25)

Each of the summands in the expression of the
RHS
of Eqn. (
A.1
) can be written as follows.

\displaystyle 2^{l-1}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},\lvert\hat{x}_{h+1,l-1}\rvert\geq T_{l-1}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=2^{l-1}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},\lvert\hat{x}_{h+1,l-1}\rvert\geq T_{l-1}|E_{h+2,d}\right]\pm 2^{l-1}\left(\delta^{\prime}+dn^{-\Omega(1)}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(26)

\displaystyle 2^{l}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},Q_{l-1}<\lvert\hat{x}_{h+1,l-1}\rvert<T_{l-1},K_{i}=1\mid E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=2^{l}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},Q_{l-1}<\lvert\hat{x}_{h+1,l-1}\rvert<T_{l-1},K_{i}=1\mid E_{h+2,d}\right]\pm 2^{l-1}(\delta^{\prime}+dn^{-\Omega(1)})

(27)

\displaystyle 2^{l-1}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},g_{l}(i_{h+1})=1,Q_{l-1}>\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l-1}\rvert\mid E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=2^{l-1}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1},Q_{l-1}>\lvert\hat{x}_{i_{h+1},l-1}\rvert\mid E_{h+2,d}\right]\pm 2^{l-1}\left(\delta^{\prime}+dn^{-\Omega(1)}\right)

(28)

Adding Eqns. (
26
) through (
28
), we have,

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{h+1}\textsf{~consist. with~}i_{h+1}}2^{l_{h+1}}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h+1}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=2^{l-1}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\mid E_{h+2,d}\right]\pm 3\cdot 2^{l-1}(\delta^{\prime}+dn^{-\Omega(1)})

(29)

Further, consider the probability term in the
RHS
of Eqn. (
A.1
). We have,

\displaystyle\text{Pr}\left[G_{h},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\mid E_{h+2,d}\right]

\displaystyle=\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\mid E_{h+2,d},i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\right]\cdot\text{Pr}\left[i_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}_{l-1}\mid E_{h+2,d}\right]

\displaystyle=2^{-(l-1)}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\mid E_{h+1,d}\right]

Substituting in Eqn. (
A.1
), we have,

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{h+1}\textsf{~consist. with~}i_{h+1}}2^{l_{h+1}}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h+1}|E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=2^{l-1}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\mid E_{h+1,d}\right]\pm 3\cdot 2^{l-1}\left(\delta^{\prime}+dn^{-\Omega(1)}\right)

\displaystyle=\text{Pr}\left[G_{h}\mid E_{h+1,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]\pm 4\cdot 2^{l-1}\left(\delta^{\prime}+dn^{-\Omega(1)}\right)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{j}\textsf{~consist. with~}i_{j}\\
\forall j\in[h+1]\end{subarray}}2^{l_{1}+\ldots+l_{h+1}}\text{Pr}\left[G_{h+1}\mid E_{h+2,d},\mathcal{G},K_{d}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{h}(1\pm 2^{\mathsf{level}(i_{j})+1}(\delta^{\prime}+dn^{-\Omega(1)})\pm 4\cdot 2^{l-1}\left(\delta^{\prime}+dn^{-\Omega(1)}\right)

which proves the property.

A.2
p
th power estimator for
\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}

Lemma 28

Let
W=\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{j}\omega(j)
where,
\{\omega(j)\}_{j=1}^{n}
is a 2d-wise independent family of randomly chosen roots of
x^{q}=1
for any integer
q>d
, where,
d\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{+}
. Then, for any
d\geq 1
,

\mathbf{E}\left[(W\overline{W})^{d}\right]\leq d!\left\lVert{a}\right\rVert_{2}^{2d}
, where

a
is the
n
-dimensional vector
(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{n})
.

Proof:

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(W\overline{W})^{d}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{n}=d}\sum_{g_{1}+\ldots+g_{n}=d}\binom{d}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}}\binom{d}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}a_{j}^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\mathbf{E}\left[\omega_{j}^{e_{j}}\overline{\omega_{j}}^{g_{j}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{n}=d}\binom{d}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}}^{2}a_{j}^{2e_{j}}

\displaystyle\leq d!\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{n}=d}\binom{d}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}}a_{j}^{2e_{j}}

\displaystyle=d!\left\lVert{a}\right\rVert_{2}^{2d}

The first step views
(W\overline{W})^{d}
as
W^{d}\overline{W}^{d}
and takes the product of the multinomial expansion for
W^{d}
and
\overline{W}^{d}
; and subsequently uses linearity of expectation. The second step follows since
\mathbf{E}\left[\omega_{j}^{e_{j}}\overline{\omega_{j}}^{g_{j}}\right]=0
unless
e_{j}=g_{j}
, in which case, it is 1.

Lemma 29
(Re-statement of Lemma
8
.)

Let
Z=\sum_{j=1}^{t}a_{j}\omega(j)\chi(j)
, where,
\{\omega(j)\}_{j=1}^{t}
is a family of random and
2d
-wise independent family roots of the equation
x^{q}=1
,
q>2d
and integral. Let
\{\chi(j)\}
be a
2d
-wise independent family of indicator variables such that
\text{Pr}\left[\chi(j)=1\right]=1/C
and is independent of the
\omega_{j}
’s. If
\left\lVert{a}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\geq 4d\left\lVert{a}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}C
, then,

\mathbf{E}\left[(Z\bar{Z})^{d}\right]\leq(2)\left(\frac{d\left\lVert{a}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{C}\right)^{d}

where,
a
is the
t
-dimensional vector
(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{t})
.

Proof:
[Proof of Lemma
8
.]
Assume
K=\left\lVert{a}\right\rVert_{\infty}
.

\mathbf{E}\left[(Z\bar{Z})^{d}\right]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{t}=d\\
e_{j}\text{'s }\geq 0\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{t}=d\\
g_{j}\text{'s }\geq 0\end{subarray}}\binom{d}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{t}}\binom{d}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{t}}\\
\cdot\prod_{u=1}^{t}a_{u}^{e_{u}+g_{u}}\mathbf{E}\left[\omega^{e_{u}}(j)\bar{\omega}^{g_{u}}(j)\right]\mathbf{E}\left[\chi^{e_{u}+g_{u}}(j)\right]~~

(by
2d
-wise independence)
.
The expectation is 0 unless
e_{u}=g_{u}
for each
u\in[t]
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(Z\bar{Z})^{d}\right]

(30)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=1}^{d}\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r}=d,e_{j}\text{'s}\geq 1}\binom{d}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r}}^{2}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{r}\leq t}\prod_{u=1}^{r}\frac{a_{i_{u}}^{2e_{u}}}{C}

\displaystyle\leq K^{2d}\sum_{r=1}^{d}\frac{1}{K^{2r}}\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r}=d}\binom{d}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r}}^{2}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{r}\leq t}\prod_{u=1}^{r}\frac{a_{i_{u}}^{2}}{C}

(since,
a_{i_{u}}^{2e_{u}}\leq K^{2e_{u}-2}a_{i_{u}}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq K^{2d}\sum_{r=1}^{d}\frac{d!}{K^{2r}}\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r}=d}\binom{d}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r}}\frac{1}{r!}\left(\frac{\left\lVert{a}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{C}\right)^{r}

(31)

\displaystyle\leq K^{2d}d!\sum_{r=1}^{d}\frac{1}{r!}\left(\frac{\left\lVert{a}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{K^{2}C}\right)^{r}\frac{d^{r}}{(d-r+1)}\cdot r^{d-r}

(32)

Eqn. (
31
) follows by observing that (i)
\sum_{1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{r}\leq t}\prod_{u=1}^{r}\frac{a_{i_{u}}^{2}}{C}\leq\frac{1}{r!}\left(\frac{\left\lVert{a}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{C}\right)^{r}
, which can be seen by expanding
\frac{1}{C^{r}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{t}a_{j}^{2}\right)^{r}
, and, (ii)
\binom{d}{e_{1},\ldots e_{r}}^{2}\leq d!\binom{d}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r}}
.

Eqn. (
32
) is obtained as follows. Let
e^{\prime}_{j}=e_{j}-1
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r}=d,e_{j}\text{'s}\geq 1}

\displaystyle\binom{d}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{d(d-1)\ldots(d-r+1)}{e_{1}e_{2}\ldots e_{r}}\right)\sum_{e^{\prime}_{1}+\ldots+e^{\prime}_{r}=d-r,e^{\prime}_{j}\text{'s}\geq 0}\binom{d-r}{e^{\prime}_{1},e^{\prime}_{2},\ldots,e^{\prime}_{r}}

Now,
\frac{d(d-1)\ldots(d-r)}{e_{1}e_{2}\ldots e_{r}}\leq\left(\frac{d^{r}}{d-r+1}\right)
, where the product
e_{1}e_{2}\ldots e_{r}
is minimized, subject to
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r}\geq 1
and
e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r}=d
, by letting
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r-1}
to be 1 and
e_{r}=d-r+1
. Also,

\sum_{e^{\prime}_{1}+\ldots+e^{\prime}_{r}=d-r,e^{\prime}_{j}\text{'s}\geq 0}\binom{d-r}{e^{\prime}_{1},e^{\prime}_{2},\ldots,e^{\prime}_{r}}=(\underbrace{1+1+\ldots+1}_{\text{ $r$ times }})^{d-r}=r^{d-r}

Combining, this gives Eqn. (
32
).

Consider the sum in Eqn. (
32
). The ratio of the
r+1
th term to the
r
th term, for
r=1,2,\ldots,d-1
, is

\frac{d\left\lVert{a}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{(r+1)K^{2}C}\cdot\left(\frac{d-r+1}{d-r}\right)\cdot\frac{(r+1)^{d-r-1}}{r^{d-r}}\geq\frac{d\left\lVert{a}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2(r+1)^{2}K^{2}C}\geq\frac{4d^{2}}{2(r+1)^{2}}\geq 2\kern 5.0pt.

Hence, the sum in Eqn. (
32
) is a geometrically increasing sequence with common ratio at least 2. The sum is therefore bounded above by 2
\times
the final term, that is,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(Z\bar{Z})^{d}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\frac{d\left\lVert{a}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{C}\right)^{d}\kern 5.0pt.

For
r\in[2s]
, let
\chi_{rij}
be an indicator variable that is 1 if

For

assuming

from table

Lemma 30

[Restatement of Lemma
9
.]Suppose

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[((X_{i}-\lvert x_{i}\rvert)^{d}(\overline{X_{i}}-\lvert x_{i}\rvert)^{d}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]\leq 2\left(\frac{dF_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({2C}\right)}{(\rho/9)C}\right)^{d},\text{ and }

\displaystyle\text{Pr}\left[\lvert X_{i}-\lvert x_{i}\rvert\rvert>2\left(\frac{dF_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({2C}\right)}{(s/9)C}\right)^{1/2}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]<2^{-2d+1}\kern 5.0pt.

[Proof of Lemma

(40)

From Eqn. (

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{i}^{d}\overline{Z_{i}}^{d}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[(X_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[X_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right])(\overline{X_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[X_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]})^{d}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\rho^{-2d}\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{r\in R(i)}\sum_{j\neq i}x_{j}\omega_{r}(j)\overline{\omega_{r}(i)}\chi_{rji}\right)^{d}\left(\sum_{r\in R(i)}\sum_{j\neq i}x_{j}\overline{\omega_{r}(j)}\omega_{r}(i)\chi_{rji}\right)^{d}\biggl|\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\rho^{-2d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sum_{r\in R(i)}\sum_{j\neq i}e_{rj}=d\\
e_{rj}\text{'s}\geq 0\end{subarray}}\binom{d}{e_{11},\ldots,e_{\rho n}}^{2}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in R(i)\\

\displaystyle=\rho^{-2d}\mathbf{E}\left[(Z^{\prime}_{i}\overline{Z^{\prime}_{i}})^{d}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(41)

from Eqn. (
39
) defining

.

(42)

and, hence from Eqn. (
A.2

(43)

Therefore,

\text{Pr}\left[\bigl\lvert X_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[X_{i}\right]\bigr\rvert>T\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]=\text{Pr}\left[\lvert Z_{i}\rvert>T\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]=\text{Pr}\left[(Z_{i}\overline{Z_{i}})^{d}>T^{2d}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]\\
\leq\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[(Z_{i}\bar{Z_{i}})^{d}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]}{T^{2d}}\leq 2\left(\frac{dF_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({2C}\right)}{(\rho/9)CT^{2}}\right)^{d}\kern 5.0pt.

The following lemma uses a standard property of
q
th roots of unity, that
\mathbf{E}\left[\omega^{v}\right]=0
, where,
\omega
is a randomly chosen root of
x^{q}=1
and
0\leq v\leq q-1
.

Lemma 31

Assume that the family
\{\omega(j)\}
are
k+1
-wise independent and randomly chosen roots of
x^{q}=1
, where,
q>k+1
is any integer. Then,
for
v=1,2\ldots,k
,
\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{i}^{v}\right]=0
.

Proof:
[Proof of Lemma
31
]
For
v=1,2,\ldots,k
and
l\in R(i)
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{li}^{v}\right]

\displaystyle=\textrm{sgn}^{v}(\hat{x}_{i})\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{\omega_{li}}^{v}\right]\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{j\neq i}x_{j}\omega_{lj}\chi_{lij}\right)^{v}\right]

\displaystyle=\textrm{sgn}^{v}(\hat{x}_{i})\cdot 0\cdot\left(\sum_{r=1}^{v}\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r}=v,e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1}\binom{v}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r}}\sum_{1\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{r}\leq n,j_{u}^{\prime}s\neq i}\frac{1}{C^{r}}\prod_{u=1}^{r}x_{j_{u}}^{e_{u}}\mathbf{E}\left[\omega_{li}^{e_{u}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=0\cdot 0=0

since, each of the terms
\mathbf{E}\left[\omega_{li}^{e_{u}}\right]=0
.

Therefore, for
v=1,2,\ldots,k
,

\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{i}^{v}\right]=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{l=1}^{s}Z_{li}\right)^{v}\right]=0
,
since, the
Z_{li}
’s are independent across the
l
’s.

Lemma 32
(Restatement of Lemma
10
)

Let
\{\omega_{r}(j)\}_{j\in[n]}
be a family of
d+1
-wise independent variables, where,
d=O(\log n)
,
s\geq 300\log(n)
and
s\geq 72d
. Further suppose that
\lvert x_{i}\rvert>8\left(\frac{F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({2C}\right)}{2C}\right)^{1/2}
.
Then, with probability
1-n^{-\Omega(1)}
,

\bigl\lvert\mathbf{E}\left[X_{i}^{p}\right]-\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\bigr\rvert\leq\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}n^{-\Omega(1)}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof:

We have,
X_{i}=\lvert x_{i}\rvert+Z_{i}
, where,
Z_{i}=\frac{1}{\rho}\sum_{r\in R(i)}Z_{ri}
and

Z_{ri}=\sum_{j\neq i}x_{j}\omega_{r}(j)\overline{\omega_{r}(i)}\chi_{rij}\textrm{sgn}(x_{i})
.
By Lemma
9
,

\text{Pr}\left[\lvert Z_{i}\rvert>2\left(\frac{dF_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({2C}\right)}{(s/9)C}\right)^{1/2}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]\leq 2^{-2d+1}\kern 5.0pt.

Since
s\geq 72d
,
(s/9)/d\geq 8
. Also, since,
d=\Theta(\log n)
,
2^{-2d+1}=n^{-\Omega(1)}
. Therefore,

\text{Pr}\left[\lvert Z_{i}\rvert>\left(\frac{F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({2C}\right)}{2C}\right)^{1/2}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]\leq n^{-\Omega(1)}\kern 5.0pt.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[X_{i}^{p}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\lvert x_{i}\rvert+Z_{i}\right)^{p}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}\mathbf{E}\left[\left(1+\frac{Z_{i}}{\lvert x_{i}\rvert}\right)^{p}\right]

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}\left(\sum_{v=0}^{k}\binom{p}{v}\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{i}^{v}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i}\right]}{\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{v}}\pm\left\lvert\binom{p}{k+1}\right\rvert 8^{-(k+1)}\right)

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}\left(1\pm\left(\frac{p}{k+1}\right)^{\lfloor p\rfloor}8^{-(k+1)}\right)

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}\left(1\pm n^{-\Omega(1)}\right)

The second to last step follows, since for
v=1,2,\ldots,k
,
\mathbf{E}\left[(Z_{i})^{v}\right]=0
, by Lemma
31
. The error term is bounded by an application of Taylor’s remainder term in the expansion of
(1+z)^{p}
, for
\lvert z\rvert\leq 1/8
. We have also used the fact that for
k>p
,
\bigl\lvert\binom{p}{k+1}\bigr\rvert\leq\left(\frac{p}{k+1}\right)^{\lceil p\rceil}
.

A.3
Analysis

For
i\in[n]
, let
x_{li}
be an indicator variable that is 1 iff
i\in\mathcal{S}_{l}
.
For uniformity of notation, let
X_{i}
denote
\lvert\hat{x}_{i}\rvert
when
l_{d}(i)=L
and otherwise, let its meaning be unchanged.

Let
z_{il}
be an indicator variable
that is 1 if
i\in\bar{G}_{l}
and 0 otherwise.

We can now write
\hat{F}_{p}
as follows.

\displaystyle\hat{F}_{p}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\bar{G}_{l}\\
l_{d}(i)<L\end{subarray}}2^{l}X_{i}^{p}=\sum_{i\in[n]}Y_{i},\text{ where, }Y_{i}=\sum_{l^{\prime}=0}^{L}2^{l^{\prime}}z_{il^{\prime}}X_{i}^{p}\kern 5.0pt.

(44)

A.4
Analysis of
ghss
sampled estimate

We now consider the analysis of the
ghss
sampled estimate.
Without loss of generality, for
l_{d}(i)=l
, let
\lvert R_{l}(i)\rvert=s
.

Let
nocollision
be defined as the event
\wedge_{i\in[n]}\textsc{nocollision}(i)
. This means that, for each
i\in[n]
, (i) if
i\in G_{l}
and
i
maps to the random substream
\mathcal{S}_{l}
, then,
\textsc{nocollision}(i)
holds at level
l_{d}(i)
, and (ii) if
i\in S_{j}
and
\textsc{nocollision}(i)
holds at the shelf indexed
j_{d}(i)
, namely the shelf at which the item is discovered (the largest shelf index).
Let
\textsc{nocollision}(J)
denote
\wedge_{j\in J}\textsc{nocollision}(j)
. Analogously, for a set
J
, define
\textsc{goodest}(J)
.
For a set
J
, let
\mathcal{H}_{J}
be the event

\mathcal{H}_{J}=\mathcal{G}\wedge\textsc{nocollision}(J)\wedge\textsc{goodest}(J)\kern 5.0pt.

Lemma 33

Let
r\in[0,\ldots,d],s\in\max(0,1-r),\ldots,d-r
and
t\in\max(0,1-r),\ldots,d-r
. Let
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r}\}\cup\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{s}\}\cup\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{t}\}\subset\text{lmargin}(G_{0})\cup_{l=1}^{L}G_{l}
such that
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r}\}
,
\{j_{1},\ldots,\allowbreak j_{s}\}
and

and

l\in[0,L]
and
r\in[2s]

\gamma=\textrm{exp}\left\{{-\Omega(s)}\right\}=n^{-\Omega(1)}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{u=1}^{r}(Y_{i_{u}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{u}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right])^{e_{u}}(\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right])^{g_{u}}\prod_{v=1}^{s}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right])^{e_{r+v}}\right.

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{\lvert A\rvert+\lvert B\rvert}(1\pm\gamma)^{(\lvert A\rvert+\lvert B\rvert)^{2}}\prod_{v\in A}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w\in B}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}

Proof:

We have,
\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{s}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right]=\lvert x_{s}\rvert^{p}(1\pm n^{-\Omega(1)}))

.
Let
\gamma=n^{-\Omega(1)}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{u=1}^{r}(Y_{i_{u}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{u}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right])^{e_{u}}(\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right])^{g_{u}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\left.\prod_{v=1}^{s}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right])^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w=1}^{t}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right])^{g_{r+w}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{v\in A}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right])\prod_{w\in B}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right])\right.

(45)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.90755pt\prod_{u=1}^{r}(Y_{i_{u}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{u}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right])^{e_{u}}(\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right])^{g_{u}}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.68121pt\left.\prod_{v\in[s]\setminus A}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right])^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]\setminus B}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right])^{g_{r+w}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J}\right]

\displaystyle=

Fact 34

Suppose
i\in\text{lmargin}(G_{0})\cup_{l^{\prime}=1}^{L}G_{l^{\prime}}
and suppose

l
is consistent with
i
and
\beta\geq 1
is an integer. Then,

\displaystyle\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p\beta}2^{l\beta}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{l}\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}\left(\frac{F_{2}}{B}\right)^{p(\beta-1)/2}2^{(1+p/2)(\beta-1)}

Proof:
[Proof of Fact
34
.]
By the definition of
\mathsf{level}(i)
,
\lvert x_{i}\rvert\leq\left(\frac{F_{2}}{(2\alpha)^{\mathsf{level}(i)-1}B}\right)^{1/2}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p\beta}2^{l\beta}=2^{l}\cdot 2^{l(\beta-1)}\cdot\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}\cdot\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p(\beta-1)}\leq 2^{l}\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}\left(\frac{F_{2}}{(2\alpha)^{\mathsf{level}(i)-1}B}\right)^{p(\beta-1)/2}2^{l(\beta-1)}

(54)

Now
-1\leq l-\mathsf{level}(i)\leq 1
and

2^{\ell}(2\alpha)^{-\ell p/2}=2^{\ell(1-(p/2)\log_{2}(2\alpha))}
, for any
\ell
.

Since,
\alpha=1-(1-2/p)\nu
, where,
\nu=0.01
,
\ln(\alpha)\geq-2(1-2/p)\nu
. Hence,

\displaystyle 1-(p/2)\log_{2}(2\alpha)

\displaystyle=1-(p/2)(1+\log_{2}(\alpha))

\displaystyle=1-p/2-\frac{p/2}{\ln 2}\ln(\alpha)

\displaystyle\leq-(p/2-1)+\frac{(p/2)}{\ln 2}(1-2/p)(2\nu))

\displaystyle=-(p/2-1)+(p/2-1)\frac{(2\nu)}{\ln 2}

\displaystyle=-(p/2-1)(1-2\nu/\ln(2))

\displaystyle<0

Hence, for any
\ell\geq 0
,
2^{\ell}(2\alpha)^{-\ell p/2}<1
. Therefore, for any integer
\beta\geq 1
, we have,

\displaystyle\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p\beta}2^{l\beta}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{l}\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}\left(\frac{F_{2}}{(2\alpha)^{\mathsf{level}(i)-1}B}\right)^{p(\beta-1)/2}2^{l(\beta-1)}

\displaystyle=2^{l}\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}\left(\frac{F_{2}}{B}\right)^{p(\beta-1)/2}\left(\frac{2^{\mathsf{level}(i)}}{(2\alpha)^{(p/2)\mathsf{level}(i)}}\right)^{\beta-1}(2\alpha)^{(p/2)(\beta-1)}2^{(l-\mathsf{level}(i))(\beta-1)}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{l}\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}\left(\frac{F_{2}}{B}\right)^{p(\beta-1)/2}2^{(1+p/2)(\beta-1)}

Lemma 35

Suppose
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r},g_{1},\ldots,g_{r}\geq 1
and
e_{r+1},\ldots,e_{r+s^{\prime}}\geq 2
and
g_{r+1},\ldots,g_{r+t}\geq 2
. Let
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r}\}\cup\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{s^{\prime}}\}\cup\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{t}\}\subset\text{lmargin}(G_{0})\cup_{l=1}^{L}G_{l}
. Let
s\geq 2\max_{u=1}^{r}(e_{u},g_{u})
and
B/C\leq 1/(72p^{2})
. Let
J^{\prime}
denote the set
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r},j_{1},\ldots,j_{s}^{\prime},k_{1},\ldots,k_{r}^{\prime}\}
. Let
\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}
denote
\mathcal{G}\wedge\textsc{nocollision}(J^{\prime})\wedge\textsc{accuest}(J^{\prime})
.
Then,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}

\displaystyle\left[\prod_{u=1}^{r}Y_{i_{u}}^{e_{u}}\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}^{g_{u}}\prod_{v\in[s^{\prime}]}Y_{j_{v}}^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]}\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}^{g_{r+v}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\leq 2e^{\sum_{u\in[r]}(e_{u}+g_{u})/8}\prod_{u=1}^{r}\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{v=1}^{s}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w=1}^{t}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 79.49744pt\cdot\left(\frac{4F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)\left(\sum_{u=1}^{r}(e_{u}+g_{u}-1)+\sum_{v\in[s]}(e_{r+v}-1)+\sum_{w\in[t]}(e_{r+w}-1)\right)}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof:
The expectations in this proof are conditioned on the conjunction of events
\mathcal{G}
,
\textsc{nocollision}(J)
and
\textsc{goodest}(J)
. For brevity, let
\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}=\mathcal{G}\wedge\textsc{nocollision}(J^{\prime})\wedge\textsc{goodest}(J^{\prime})
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\prod_{u=1}^{r}Y_{i_{u}}^{e_{u}}\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}^{g_{u}}\prod_{v\in[{s^{\prime}}]}Y_{j_{v}}^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]}\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}^{g_{r+w}}\right|\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{u=1}^{r}\left(\sum_{l_{u}\text{ consist. w. }i_{u}}2^{l_{u}}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}X_{i_{u}}^{p}\right)^{e_{u}}\left(\sum_{l_{u}\text{ consist. w. }i_{u}}2^{l_{u}}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}\overline{X_{i_{u}}}^{p}\right)^{g_{u}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\prod_{v\in[{s^{\prime}}]}\left(\sum_{l^{\prime}_{v}\text{ consist. w. }j_{v}}2^{l^{\prime}_{v}}z_{j_{v}l^{\prime}_{v}}X_{j_{v}}^{p}\right)^{e_{r+v}}\left.\prod_{w\in[t]}\left(\sum_{\ell_{w}\text{ consist. w. }k_{w}}2^{\ell_{w}}z_{k_{w}\ell_{w}}\overline{X_{k_{w}}}^{p}\right)^{g_{r+w}}\right|\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]

(55)

Consider one of the power terms in Eqn. (
A.4
), say,
\left(\sum_{l_{u}\text{ consist. w. }i_{u}}2^{l_{u}}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}X_{i_{u}}^{p}\right)^{e_{u}}
. Note that for each
i_{u}\in\text{lmargin}(G_{0})\cup_{l=1}^{L}G_{l}
,
i_{u}
is sampled into at most one group
\bar{G}_{l}
, for
l\in[0,L]
. Since,
z_{i_{u},l}
is the indicator variable that is 1 iff
i_{u}
is sampled into
\bar{G}_{l}
, therefore, for
z_{i_{u},l}=1
for at most one
l\in[0,L]
. Hence, for
l\neq l^{\prime}
,
z_{i_{u},l}z_{i_{u},l^{\prime}}=0
. Now,

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l_{u}\text{ consist. w. }i_{u}}2^{l_{u}}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}X_{i_{u}}^{p}\right)^{e_{u}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sum_{l~\textsf{~consist. with~}~i_{u}}h_{l}=e_{u}\\
h_{l}\text{'s}\geq 0\end{subarray}}~~\prod_{l~\textsf{~consist. with~}~i_{u}}\left(2^{l}z_{i_{u},l}X_{i_{u}}^{p}\right)^{h_{l}}

In the product term
\prod_{l~\textsf{~consist. with~}~i_{u}}\left(2^{l}z_{i_{u},l}X_{i_{u}}^{p}\right)^{h_{l}}
, if
h_{l}
and
h_{l^{\prime}}
are both non-zero for some
l\neq l^{\prime}
, then,
z_{i_{u},l}^{h_{l}}z_{i_{u},l^{\prime}}^{h_{l^{\prime}}}=z_{i_{u},l}z_{i_{u},l^{\prime}}=0
, and therefore the product term is 0. Hence, the only contribution comes from the diagonal terms, that is

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l_{u}\text{ consist. w. }i_{u}}2^{l_{u}}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}X_{i_{u}}^{p}\right)^{e_{u}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l_{u}~\textsf{~consist. with~}~i_{u}}2^{l_{u}e_{u}}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}X_{i_{u}}^{pe_{u}}

\displaystyle=X_{i_{u}}^{pe_{u}}\sum_{l_{u}\textsf{~consist. with~}~i_{u}}2^{l_{u}e_{u}}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}\kern 5.0pt.

Substituting in Eqn. (
A.4
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}

\displaystyle\left[\prod_{u=1}^{r}\left(\sum_{l_{u}\text{ consist. w. }i_{u}}2^{l_{u}}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}X_{i_{u}}^{p}\right)^{e_{u}}\left(\sum_{l_{u}\text{ consist. w. }i_{u}}2^{l_{u}}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}\overline{X_{i_{u}}}^{p}\right)^{g_{u}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\prod_{v\in[{s^{\prime}}]}\left(\sum_{l^{\prime}_{v}\text{ consist. w. }j_{v}}2^{l^{\prime}_{v}}z_{j_{v}l^{\prime}_{v}}X_{j_{v}}^{p}\right)^{e_{r+v}}\left.\prod_{w\in[t]}\left(\sum_{\ell_{w}\text{ consist. w. }k_{w}}2^{\ell_{w}}z_{k_{w}\ell_{w}}\overline{X_{k_{w}}}^{p}\right)^{g_{r+w}}\right|\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{u=1}^{r}X_{i_{u}}^{pe_{u}}\overline{X_{i_{u}}}^{pg_{u}}\prod_{v=1}^{s^{\prime}}X_{j_{v}}^{pe_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]}\overline{X_{k_{w}}}^{pg_{r+w}}\prod_{u=1}^{r}\sum_{l_{u}\text{ consist. w. }i_{u}}2^{l_{u}(e_{u}+g_{u})}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\left.\left.\prod_{v=1}^{s^{\prime}}\sum_{l^{\prime}_{v}\text{ consist. w. }j_{v}}2^{l^{\prime}_{v}e_{r+v}}z_{j_{v}l^{\prime}_{v}}\prod_{w=1}^{t}\sum_{\ell_{w}\text{ consist. w. }k_{w}}2^{\ell_{w}g_{r+w}}z_{k_{w}\ell_{w}}\right|\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(56)

For any
i
, conditional on
\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}
,
X_{i}=\lvert x_{i}\rvert+Z_{i}=\lvert x_{i}\rvert\left(1+\frac{Z_{i}}{\lvert x_{i}\rvert}\right)
, where
Z_{i}
is given by Eqns. (
35
) and (
36
). Then, Eqn. (
A.4
) equals

\displaystyle=\prod_{u=1}^{r}\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert^{p(e_{u}+g_{u})}\prod_{v=1}^{s^{\prime}}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{pe_{r+v}}\prod_{w=1}^{t}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{pg_{r+t}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.45377pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{u}\text{ consist. w. }i_{u},u=1,\ldots,r\\
l^{\prime}_{v}\text{ consist. w. }j_{v},v\in[{s^{\prime}}]\\
\ell_{w}\text{ con{s'}ist. w. }k_{w},w\in[t]\end{subarray}}2^{\sum_{u=1}^{r}{l_{u}(e_{u}+g_{u})}+\sum_{v=1}^{s^{\prime}}l^{\prime}_{v}e_{r+v}+\sum_{w=1}^{t}\ell_{w}g_{r+w}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{u=1}^{r}\left(1+\frac{Z_{i_{u}}}{\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert}\right)^{pe_{u}}\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{i_{u}}}}{\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert}\right)^{pg_{u}}\prod_{v=1}^{s^{\prime}}\left(1+\frac{Z_{j_{v}}}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert}\right)^{pe_{r+v}}\prod_{w=1}^{t}\left(1+\frac{Z_{k_{w}}}{\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert}\right)^{pg_{r+w}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 43.36243pt\left|\bigwedge_{u=1}^{r}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}=1\bigwedge_{v=1}^{t}z_{j_{v}l^{\prime}_{v}}=1\bigwedge_{w=1}^{t}z_{k_{w}\ell_{w}}=1,\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right.\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 43.36243pt\cdot\text{Pr}\left[\bigwedge_{u=1}^{r}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}=1\bigwedge_{v=1}^{t}z_{j_{v}l^{\prime}_{v}}=1\bigwedge_{w=1}^{t}z_{k_{w}\ell_{w}}=1,\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(57)

We will denote the expression in the expectation as
P(I,J,K,e,g)
, where,
I=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r}\},J=\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{s^{\prime}}\}
and
K=\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{t}\}
.
e
denotes the vector
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+{s^{\prime}}})
and
g
denotes the vector
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{r+t})
, where,
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r}\geq 1
,
g_{1},\ldots,g_{r}\geq 1
, and
e_{r+1},\ldots,e_{r+{s^{\prime}}},g_{r+1},\ldots,g_{r+t}\geq 2
. Then, Eqn. (
A.4
) may be written as

\displaystyle=\prod_{u=1}^{r}\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert^{p(e_{u}+g_{u})}\prod_{v=1}^{s^{\prime}}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{pe_{r+v}}\prod_{w=1}^{t}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{pg_{r+t}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{u}\text{ consist. w. }i_{u},u=1,\ldots,r\\
l^{\prime}_{v}\text{ consist. w. }j_{v},v\in[{s^{\prime}}]\\
\ell_{w}\text{ con{s'}ist. w. }k_{w},w\in[t]\end{subarray}}2^{\sum_{u=1}^{r}{l_{u}(e_{u}+g_{u})}+\sum_{v=1}^{s^{\prime}}l^{\prime}_{v}e_{r+v}+\sum_{w=1}^{t}\ell_{w}g_{r+w}}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.58878pt\mathbf{E}\left[P(I,J,K,e,g)\left|\bigwedge_{u=1}^{r}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}=1\bigwedge_{v=1}^{t}z_{j_{v}l^{\prime}_{v}}=1\bigwedge_{w=1}^{t}z_{k_{w}\ell_{w}}=1,\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right.\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\text{Pr}\left[\bigwedge_{u=1}^{r}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}=1\bigwedge_{v=1}^{t}z_{j_{v}l^{\prime}_{v}}=1\bigwedge_{w=1}^{t}z_{k_{w}\ell_{w}}=1,\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

By
\textsc{nocollision}(J)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[P(I,J,K,e,g)\mid\left|\bigwedge_{u=1}^{r}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}=1\bigwedge_{v=1}^{t}z_{j_{v}l^{\prime}_{v}}=1\bigwedge_{w=1}^{t}z_{k_{w}\ell_{w}}=1,\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right.\right]

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\prod_{u=1}^{r}\left(1+\frac{Z_{i_{u}}}{\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert}\right)^{pe_{u}}\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{i_{u}}}}{\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert}\right)^{pg_{u}}\prod_{v=1}^{s^{\prime}}\left(1+\frac{Z_{j_{v}}}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert}\right)^{pe_{r+v}}\prod_{w=1}^{t}\left(1+\frac{Z_{k_{w}}}{\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert}\right)^{pg_{r+w}}\right|\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\left.\bigwedge_{u=1}^{r}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}=1\bigwedge_{v=1}^{t}z_{j_{v}l^{\prime}_{v}}=1\bigwedge_{w=1}^{t}z_{k_{w}\ell_{w}}=1,\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{u=1}^{r}\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\left(1+\frac{Z_{i_{u}}}{\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert}\right)^{pe_{u}}\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{i_{u}}}}{\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert}\right)^{pg_{u}}\right\rvert z_{i_{u},l_{u}}=1,\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot\prod_{v=1}^{s^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\left(1+\frac{Z_{j_{v}}}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert}\right)^{pe_{r+v}}\right|z_{j_{v},l^{\prime}_{v}}=1,\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot\prod_{w=1}^{t}\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\left(1+\frac{Z_{k_{w}}}{\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert}\right)^{pg_{r+w}}\right|z_{k_{w},\ell_{w}}=1,\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]\left(1\pm n^{-\Omega(1)}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(58)

We now consider
\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\left(1+\frac{Z_{i_{u}}}{\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert}\right)^{pe_{u}}\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{i_{u}}}}{\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert}\right)^{pg_{u}}\right\rvert z_{i_{u},l_{u}}=1,\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]
. Then, since,
z_{i_{u},l_{u}}=1
, we have
\frac{\lvert Z_{i_{u}}\rvert}{\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert}\leq(B/C)^{1/2}\leq 1/(8p)
with probability
1-n^{-\Omega(1)}
. Hence,

\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\left(1+\frac{Z_{i_{u}}}{\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert}\right)^{pe_{u}}\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{i_{u}}}}{\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert}\right)^{pg_{u}}\right\rvert\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]\\
\leq\left(1+\frac{\lvert Z_{i_{u}}\rvert}{\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert}\right)^{pe_{u}}\left(1+\frac{\lvert Z_{i_{u}}\rvert}{\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert}\right)^{pg_{u}}\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{8p}\right)^{p(e_{u}+g_{u})}\kern 5.0pt.

with probability
1-n^{-\Omega(1)}
.

Now consider
\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\left(1+\frac{Z_{j_{v}}}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert}\right)^{pe_{r+v}}\right|z_{j_{v},l^{\prime}_{v}}=1,\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]
, for a fixed
v\in[s^{\prime}]
. Let
c_{i}=\binom{pe_{r+v}}{i}
, for
i=1,2,\ldots,k
. Since,
\frac{\lvert Z_{j_{v}}\rvert}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert}\leq 1/8
with probability
1-n^{-\Omega(1)}
, we have by the Taylor’s series expansion of
\left(1+\frac{Z_{j_{v}}}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert}\right)^{pe_{r+v}}
up to
k
terms (and using
k+1
-wise independence of the family
\{\omega_{lr}(i)\}_{i\in[n]}
), that

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{Z_{j_{v}}}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert}\right)^{pe_{r+v}}=\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}c_{i}\frac{Z_{j_{v}}^{i}}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{i}}+c_{k}\frac{Z_{j_{v}}^{{}^{\prime}k}}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{k}}

where,
\lvert Z^{\prime}_{j_{v}}\rvert\leq\lvert Z_{j_{v}}\rvert
.

Taking expectation, and using the fact that
\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{j_{v}}^{i}\right]=0
, for
r=1,2,\ldots,k-1
, and since,
\lvert Z^{\prime}_{j_{v}}\rvert\leq\lvert Z_{j_{v}}\rvert\leq(1/(8p))\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert
, with probability
1-n^{-\Omega(1)}
, given that
z_{j_{v},l^{\prime}_{v}}=1
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(1+\frac{Z_{j_{v}}}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert}\right)^{pe_{r+v}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}c_{r}\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{j_{v}}^{r}\mid H\right]}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{r}}+c_{k}\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{j_{v}}^{{}^{\prime}k}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{k}}=1+c_{k}\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{j_{v}}^{{}^{\prime}k}\mid\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{k}}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\lvert c_{k}\rvert\frac{\lvert Z_{j_{v}}^{{}^{\prime}k}\rvert}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{k}}\leq 1+\lvert c_{k}\rvert\frac{\lvert Z_{j_{v}}^{k}\rvert}{\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{k}}\leq 1+\lvert c_{k}\rvert(8p)^{-k}\leq 1+n^{-\Omega(1)}

since,
c_{k}=\bigl\lvert\binom{pe_{r+v}}{k}\bigr\rvert\leq\left(\frac{pe_{r+v}}{k}\right)^{\lceil p\rceil}<1
, assuming
k\geq 2pe_{r+v}
.

Substituting in Eqn. (
A.4
) we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[P(I,J,K,e,g)\mid\bigwedge_{u=1}^{r}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}=1\bigwedge_{v=1}^{t}z_{j_{v}l^{\prime}_{v}}=1\bigwedge_{w=1}^{t}z_{k_{w}\ell_{w}}=1,\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{8p}\right)^{p\sum_{u\in[r]}(e_{u}+g_{u})}\left(1+n^{-\Omega(1)}\right)^{s^{\prime}+t}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\sum_{u\in[r]}(e_{u}+g_{u})/8}\left(1+n^{-\Omega(1)}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(59)

Substituting Eqn. (
A.4
) in Eqn. (
A.4
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\prod_{u=1}^{r}Y_{i_{u}}^{e_{u}}\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}^{g_{u}}\prod_{v\in[s]}Y_{j_{v}}^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]}\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}^{g_{r+v}}\right\rvert\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=e^{\sum_{u\in[r]}(e_{u}+g_{u})/8}\left(1+n^{-\Omega(1)}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{u}\text{ consist. w. }i_{u},u=1,\ldots,r\\
l^{\prime}_{v}\text{ consist. w. }j_{v},v\in[s]\\
\ell_{w}\text{ consist. w. }k_{w},w\in[t]\end{subarray}}2^{\sum_{u=1}^{r}{l_{u}(e_{u}+g_{u})}+\sum_{v=1}^{s}l^{\prime}_{u}+\sum_{w=1}^{t}\ell_{w}}

\displaystyle\prod_{u=1}^{r}\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert^{p(e_{u}+g_{u})}\prod_{v=1}^{s}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{pe_{r+v}}\prod_{w=1}^{t}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{pg_{r+t}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\text{Pr}\left[\left.\bigwedge_{u=1}^{r}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}=1\bigwedge_{v=1}^{t}z_{j_{v}l^{\prime}_{v}}=1\bigwedge_{w=1}^{t}z_{k_{w}\ell_{w}}=1\right|\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(60)

By Fact
34
, we have, for any
i\in\text{lmargin}(G_{0})\cup_{l=1}^{L}G_{l}
,
l
consistent with
i
and
\beta\geq 1
,

\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p\beta}2^{\beta l}\leq 2^{l}\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}\left(\frac{F_{2}}{B}\right)^{p(\beta-1)/2}2^{(p/2+1)(\beta-1)}=2^{l}\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}\left(\frac{2^{1+2/p}F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)(\beta-1)}\kern 5.0pt.

Thus,

\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert^{p(e_{u}+g_{u})}2^{(e_{u}+g_{u})l_{u}}\leq\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert^{p}2^{l_{u}}\left(\frac{2^{1+2/p}F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)(e_{u}+g_{u}-1)}

and

\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{pe_{r+v}}2^{e_{r+v}l^{\prime}_{v}}\leq\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}2^{l^{\prime}_{v}}\left(\frac{2^{1+2/p}F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)(e_{r+v}-1)}\kern 5.0pt.

Using this, Eqn. (
A.4
) is upper bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\prod_{u=1}^{r}Y_{i_{u}}^{e_{u}}\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}^{g_{u}}\prod_{v\in[s^{\prime}]}Y_{j_{v}}^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]}\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}^{g_{r+v}}\right\rvert\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq e^{\sum_{u\in[r]}(e_{u}+g_{u})/8}\left(1+n^{-\Omega(1)}\right)\prod_{u=1}^{r}\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{v=1}^{s}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w=1}^{t}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\left(\frac{2^{1+2/p}F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)\left(\sum_{u=1}^{r}(e_{u}+g_{u}-1)+\sum_{v\in[s]}(e_{r+v}-1)+\sum_{w\in[t]}(e_{r+w}-1)\right)}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{u}\text{ consist. w. }i_{u},u=1,\ldots,r\\
l^{\prime}_{v}\text{ consist. w. }j_{v},v\in[s]\\
\ell_{w}\text{ consist. w. }k_{w},w\in[t]\end{subarray}}2^{\sum_{u\in[r]}l_{u}+\sum_{v\in[s]}l_{v}+\sum_{w\in[t]}l_{w}}

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt\text{Pr}\left[\bigwedge_{u=1}^{r}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}=1\bigwedge_{v=1}^{t}z_{j_{v}l^{\prime}_{v}}=1\bigwedge_{w=1}^{t}z_{k_{w}\ell_{w}}=1\mid\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]

(61)

Recall that for brevity purposes, in this proof, we have extended the event
\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}
to also include
\textsc{nocollision}(J)
and
\textsc{goodest}(J)
. Then,
by Lemma
7
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{u}\text{ consist. w. }i_{u},u=1,\ldots,r\\
l^{\prime}_{v}\text{ consist. w. }j_{v},v\in[s]\\
\ell_{w}\text{ consist. w. }k_{w},w\in[t]\end{subarray}}2^{\sum_{u\in[r]}l_{u}+\sum_{v\in[s]}l_{v}+\sum_{w\in[t]}l_{w}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot\text{Pr}\left[\bigwedge_{u=1}^{r}z_{i_{u}l_{u}}=1\bigwedge_{v=1}^{t}z_{j_{v}l^{\prime}_{v}}=1\bigwedge_{w=1}^{t}z_{k_{w}\ell_{w}}=1\mid\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]=1\pm n^{-\Omega(1)}

Substituting in Eqn. (
A.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\prod_{u=1}^{r}Y_{i_{u}}^{e_{u}}\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}^{g_{u}}\prod_{v\in[s^{\prime}]}Y_{j_{v}}^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]}\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}^{g_{r+v}}\right\rvert\mathcal{H}_{J^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq e^{\sum_{u\in[r]}(e_{u}+g_{u})/8}\left(1+n^{-\Omega(1)}\right)^{2}\prod_{u=1}^{r}\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{v=1}^{s}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w=1}^{t}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\left(\frac{2^{1+2/p}F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)\left(\sum_{u=1}^{r}(e_{u}+g_{u}-1)+\sum_{v\in[s]}(e_{r+v}-1)+\sum_{w\in[t]}(e_{r+w}-1)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{\sum_{u\in[r]}(e_{u}+g_{u})/8}\prod_{u=1}^{r}\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{v=1}^{s}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w=1}^{t}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\left(\frac{4F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)\left(\sum_{u=1}^{r}(e_{u}+g_{u}-1)+\sum_{v\in[s]}(e_{r+v}-1)+\sum_{w\in[t]}(e_{r+w}-1)\right)}

since,
p\geq 2
and
2^{1+2/p}\leq 2^{2}=4
.

Lemma 36

Let
X
be a non-negative real random variable with expectation
\mathbf{E}\left[X\right]=\mu
. Then, for any integer
e\geq 2
,
\mathbf{E}\left[(X-\mu)^{e}\right]\leq\mathbf{E}\left[X^{e}\right]+\mu^{e}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof:
Let
p_{x}=\text{Pr}\left[X=x\right]
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(X-\mu)^{e}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{x}p_{x}(x-\mu)^{e}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\leq\mu}p_{x}(x-\mu)^{e}+\sum_{x>\mu}p_{x}(x-\mu)^{e}

(62)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{x\leq\mu}p_{x}(\mu-x)^{e}+\sum_{x>\mu}p_{x}x^{e}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{x\leq\mu}p_{x}\mu^{e}+\mathbf{E}\left[X^{e}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{e}+\mathbf{E}\left[X^{e}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Step 1 follows from the definition of expectation. Step 2 separates the summation into the ranges
x\leq\mu
and
x>\mu
. Step 3 uses the fact that in the range
x\leq\mu
,
\mu-x\leq 0
and therefore for any odd
e
,
(\mu-x)^{e}\leq(x-\mu)^{e}
. For even
e
, the two powers
(\mu-x)^{e}
and
(x-\mu)^{e}
are the same. Hence for all
e\geq 2
,
(\mu-x)^{e}\leq(x-\mu)^{e}
. Further, for
x\geq\mu
,
(x-\mu)^{e}\leq x^{e}
, since,
x\geq 0
by virtue of
X
being a non-negative random variable.
Step 3 follows from the facts that (i) in the range
x\leq\mu
,
0\leq(\mu-x)\leq\mu
and hence,
(\mu-x)^{e}\leq\mu^{e}
, and, (ii) by non-negativity of
X
,
\sum_{x>\mu}p_{x}x^{e}\leq\sum_{x\geq 0}p_{x}x^{e}=\mathbf{E}\left[X^{e}\right]
. The final step follows since
\sum_{x\leq\mu}p_{x}\mu^{e}=\mu^{e}\text{Pr}\left[X\leq\mu\right]\leq\mu^{e}
.

Recall that
G^{\prime}=\text{lmargin}(G_{0})\cup_{l=1}^{L}G_{l}
.

Lemma 37

Let
j\in G^{\prime}
. Then, the following expectation is real and is bounded above as follows.

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e}_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq(1+\delta^{\prime\prime})(\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]+\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}(1+\delta^{\prime}))

where,
\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime}\in\min(O(\delta),n^{-\Omega(1)})
.

Proof:
By definition,
Y_{j}=\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{jl}X_{j}^{p}
, where,
z_{jl}=1
iff
i\in\bar{G_{l}}
. Taking expectation of
Y_{j}
,

\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}\mathbf{E}\left[z_{jl}X_{j}^{p}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]
. Note that
\mathbf{E}_{{\omega}}\left[{X_{j}^{p}\mid\mathcal{H}}\right]=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p}
as discussed earlier. This does not depend on the hash functions
g_{1},\ldots,g_{L}
which determine
z_{jl}
. Therefore,

\mathbf{E}\left[z_{jl}X_{j}^{p}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\mathbf{E}\left[z_{jl}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p}
. Thus, we have,

\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{jl}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p}(1\pm 2^{l}\min(O(\delta),n^{-\Omega(1)}))

where, the last step follows from Lemma
24
. In the following, we will refer to
(1\pm 2^{l}\min(O(\delta),n^{-\Omega(1)}))
as
1\pm\delta^{\prime}
, where,
\delta^{\prime}=\min(O(\delta),n^{-\Omega(1)}))
.

Therefore, letting
c_{k}=\binom{e_{j}}{k}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\sum_{j=0}^{k}c_{k}\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{k}\mid\mathcal{H}\right](-1)^{e_{j}-k}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}-k)}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})^{e_{j}-k}\kern 5.0pt.

(63)

Now,
\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{k}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=1
, if
k=0
. Otherwise, for
k\geq 1
, note that

\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{jl}X_{j}^{p}\right)^{k}=\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{kl}z_{jl}X_{j}^{pk}\kern 5.0pt.

Taking expectations of both sides, and noting that
\mathbf{E}_{{\omega}}\left[{X_{j}^{pk}}\right]=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pk}
and the expectation of the
z_{jl}
’s is independent of
\omega
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{jl}X_{j}^{p}\right)^{k}\right]=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pk}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{kl}z_{jl}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Substituting in Eqn. (
63
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\sum_{j=0}^{k}c_{k}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pk}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{kl}z_{jl}\right](-1)^{e_{j}-k}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}-k)}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})^{e_{j}-k}\kern 5.0pt.

Define the random variable
W_{j}=\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{jl}
. Then, the above expectation can be written as follows.

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[((Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k}c_{k}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}\mathbf{E}\left[W_{j}^{k}\right](-1)^{e_{j}-k}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}-k)}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})^{e_{j}-k}

\displaystyle=(1\pm\delta^{\prime\prime})\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}\mathbf{E}\left[(W_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[W_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right],\text{ where, $0\leq\delta^{\prime\prime}\leq\min(O(\delta),n^{-\Omega(1)})$}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\delta^{\prime\prime})\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}(\mathbf{E}\left[W_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]+\mathbf{E}\left[W_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]^{e_{j}})

(64)

Now,
\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}\mathbf{E}\left[W_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]
, by calculations as done above. Similarly,

\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}(\mathbf{E}\left[W_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{j}}=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})^{e_{j}}
. Substituting in Eqn. (
63
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[((Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq(1+\delta^{\prime\prime})(\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]+\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}(1+\delta^{\prime}))\kern 5.0pt.

Lemma
38
presents an approximation of the central moment
\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]
in terms of the (non-central) moment
\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]
.

Lemma 38

Let
j\in G^{\prime}
and
e_{j}
be an integer
\geq 0
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq(2+\delta^{\prime})\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

where,
\delta^{\prime}\leq n^{-\Omega(1)}
.

Proof:

We begin with the statement of Lemma
37
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e}_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq(1+\delta^{\prime\prime})(\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]+\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}(1+\delta^{\prime}))

(65)

where,
\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime}\leq n^{-\Omega(1)}
. We will now attempt to lower bound the term
\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]
in terms of
\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}
.
We will follow calculations similar to those in the proof of Lemma
37
. Firstly, if
e_{j}=0
, then,
\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\right]=1=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}
. So now assume that
e_{j}\geq 1
and integral.

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}\mathbf{E}\left[\biggl(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{jl}\biggr)^{e_{j}}\right]=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{le_{j}}z_{jl}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(66)

We write
2^{le_{j}}z_{jl}=2^{le_{j}-l}\cdot 2^{l}z_{jl}
, for
l\textsf{~consist. with~}j
. Let
l\textsf{~consist. with~}j
.

Case 1:
If
j\in\text{mid}(G_{r})
, then, the only value of
l
consistent with
j
is
r
. In this case,
2^{le_{j}}z_{jl}=2^{r(e_{j}-1)}\cdot 2^{l}z_{jl}
.

Case 2:
If
j\in\text{rmargin}(G_{r})
, then,
2^{le_{j}}z_{jl}\geq 2^{(r-1)(e_{j}-1)}\cdot 2^{l}z_{jl}
.

Case 3:
If
j\in\text{lmargin}(G_{r})
, then,
2^{le_{j}}z_{jl}\geq 2^{r(e_{j}-1)}\cdot 2^{l}z_{jl}
.

Now
j\in\text{lmargin}(G_{0})\cup_{l=1}^{L}G_{l}
. If
j\in G_{r}
and
r\in[L]
, then, for any
l
consistent with
j
,
2^{le_{j}}z_{jl}\geq 2^{(r-1)(e_{j}-1)}\cdot 2^{l}z_{jl}
. In this case,

\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{le_{j}}z_{jl}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\geq 2^{(r-1)(e_{j}-1)}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{jl}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=2^{(r-1)(e_{j}-1)}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})\geq 1-\delta^{\prime}

by Lemma
24
, and since,
r\geq 1
and
e_{j}\geq 1
.

If
j\in\text{lmargin}(G_{0})
, then, for any
l
consistent with
j
,
2^{le_{j}}z_{jl}\geq 2^{0(e_{j}-1)}\cdot 2^{l}z_{jl}=2^{l}z_{jl}
. In this case,
\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{le_{j}}z_{jl}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=1-\delta^{\prime}
, by Lemma
24
. Therefore, in all cases, for
j\in\text{lmargin}(G_{0})\cup_{l=1}^{L}G_{l}
, we have,

\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{le_{j}}z_{jl}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\geq 1-\delta^{\prime}\kern 5.0pt.

From Eqn. (
66
) for
e_{j}\geq 1
we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\geq(1-\delta^{\prime})\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}\kern 5.0pt.

Substituting in Eqn. (
65
) we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e}_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1+\delta^{\prime\prime})(\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]+\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}(1+\delta^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\leq(1+\delta^{\prime\prime})(1+(1-\delta^{\prime})^{-1})\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=(2+\delta^{\prime\prime\prime})\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

where,
\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}\leq n^{-\Omega(1)})
.

Note that when
e_{j}=0
,
\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=1=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{pe_{j}}
, and the
RHS
above becomes
(1+\delta^{\prime\prime})(2+\delta^{\prime})=(2+\delta^{\prime\prime\prime})\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]
.

Lemma 39

Let
j\in\text{lmargin}(G_{0})\cup_{l=1}^{L}G_{l}
. Then, for any
e_{j},g_{j}\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{j}}(\overline{Y_{j}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq\left(\frac{2(1+\delta^{\prime})}{1-\delta^{\prime}}\right)^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\overline{Y_{j}}^{g_{j}}\right]

where,
\delta^{\prime}=n^{-\Omega(1)}
.

Proof:
We have
Y_{j}=\sum_{l_{j}\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{lj}X_{j}^{p}
. Further,
\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})
, where,
\delta^{\prime}\leq n^{-\Omega(1)}
. Let
c_{k}=\binom{e_{j}}{k}
and
d_{k}=\binom{g_{j}}{k}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{j}}(\overline{Y_{j}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{lj}X_{j}^{p}-\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})\right)^{e_{j}}\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{lj}X_{j}^{p}-\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})\right)^{g_{j}}\right]

\displaystyle=\textbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{k=0}^{e_{j}}c_{k}\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{lj}X_{j}^{p}\right)^{k}(-1)^{e_{j}-k}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}-k)}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})^{p(e_{j}-k)}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\left.\left(\sum_{k^{\prime}=0}^{g_{j}}d_{k}\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{jl}X_{j}^{p}\right)^{k^{\prime}}(1-)^{g_{j}-k^{\prime}}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(g_{j}-k^{\prime})}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})^{p(e_{j}-k^{\prime})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0,0}^{e_{j},g_{j}}c_{k}d_{k^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{lj}X_{j}^{p}\right)^{k}\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{jl}X_{j}^{p}\right)^{k^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt(-1)^{e_{j}+g_{j}-k-k^{\prime}}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}+g_{j}-k-k^{\prime})}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})^{e_{j}+g_{j}-k-k^{\prime}}\kern 5.0pt.

(67)

Since,
z_{lj}z_{l^{\prime}j}=0
, for any distinct
l,l^{\prime}
, we have for any fixed
0\leq k\leq e_{j}
and
0\leq k^{\prime}\leq g_{j}
that,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{lj}X_{j}^{p}\right)^{k}\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{jl}X_{j}^{p}\right)^{k^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{lk}z_{lj}^{k}X_{j}^{pk}\right)\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{lk^{\prime}}z_{jl}^{k^{\prime}}\overline{X_{j}}^{pk^{\prime}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l(k+k^{\prime})}z_{lj}^{k+k^{\prime}}X_{j}^{pk}\overline{X_{j}}^{pk^{\prime}}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(68)

Let
a^{pk}_{r}=\binom{pk}{r}
and
b_{r^{\prime}}^{pk^{\prime}}
denote
\binom{pk^{\prime}}{r^{\prime}}
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\omega}}\left[{X_{j}^{pk}\overline{X_{j}}^{pk^{\prime}}\mid\mathcal{H}}\right]

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(k+k^{\prime})}\mathbf{E}_{{\omega}}\left[{\left(1+\frac{Z_{j}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert}\right)^{pk}\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{j}}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert}\right)^{pk^{\prime}}\mid\mathcal{H}}\right]

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(k+k^{\prime})}\sum_{r,r^{\prime}=0}^{pk,pk^{\prime}}a^{pk}_{r}b^{pk^{\prime}}_{r^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{{\omega}}\left[{Z_{j}^{r}\overline{Z_{j}}^{r}}\right]\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{-(r+r^{\prime})}\kern 5.0pt.

(69)

Note that the expression for
Z_{j}^{r}\overline{Z_{j}}^{r^{\prime}}
has the multiplicative term
\omega_{j}^{r}\overline{\omega_{j}}^{r^{\prime}}
. If
r\neq r^{\prime}
, then, upon taking expectation with respect to all the various
\omega_{i}
’s, in particular, we have,
\mathbf{E}_{{\omega_{j}}}\left[{\omega_{j}^{r}\overline{\omega_{j}}^{r^{\prime}}}\right]=0
and therefore that
\mathbf{E}_{{|omega}}\left[{Z_{j}^{r}\overline{Z_{j}}^{r^{\prime}}}\right]=0
. Thus, Eqn. (
A.4
) is equivalent to the following expression where
r=r^{\prime}
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\omega}}\left[{X_{j}^{pk}\overline{X_{j}}^{pk^{\prime}}\mid\mathcal{H}}\right]=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(k+k^{\prime})}\sum_{r}a^{pk}_{r}b^{pk^{\prime}}_{r^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{{\omega}}\left[{\frac{\lvert Z_{j}\rvert^{2r}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{2r}}\mid\mathcal{H}}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(70)

It follows that the expectation term in the above equation is real and non-negative.

Let
\mathbf{E}_{{h}}\left[{\cdot}\right]
denote the expectation with respect to the hash functions and
\mathbf{E}_{{\omega}}\left[{\cdot}\right]
denote the expectation with respect to the complex roots of unity sketches (i.e., the remaining random bits).
Substituting Eqn (
70
) in Eqn. (
A.4
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{{\omega}}\left[{X_{j}^{pk}\overline{X_{j}}^{pk^{\prime}}\mid\mathcal{H}}\right]

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(k+k^{\prime})}\sum_{r=0}^{\min(pk,pk^{\prime})}a_{r}^{pk}b_{r}^{pk^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}_{{\omega}}\left[{\frac{\lvert Z_{j}\rvert^{2r}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{2r}}}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Substituting the above equation in Eqn. (
A.4
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{lj}X_{j}^{p}\right)^{k}\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{jl}X_{j}^{p}\right)^{k^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l(k+k^{\prime})}z_{lj}^{k+k^{\prime}}X_{j}^{pk}\overline{X_{j}}^{pk^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(k+k^{\prime})}\sum_{r=0}^{\min(pk,pk^{\prime})}a_{r}^{pk}b_{r}^{pk^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l(k+k^{\prime})}z_{lj}^{k+k^{\prime}}\frac{\lvert Z_{j}\rvert^{2r}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{2r}}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Substituting this equation in Eqn. (
A.4
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{j}}(\overline{Y_{j}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0,0}^{e_{j},g_{j}}c_{k}d_{k^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{lj}X_{j}^{p}\right)^{k}\left(\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l}z_{jl}X_{j}^{p}\right)^{k^{\prime}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt(-1)^{e_{j}+g_{j}-k-k^{\prime}}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}+g_{j}-k-k^{\prime})}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})^{e_{j}+g_{j}-k-k^{\prime}}\kern 5.0pt.

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0,0}^{e_{j},g_{j}}c_{k}d_{k^{\prime}}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(k+k^{\prime})}\sum_{r=0}^{\min(pk,pk^{\prime})}a_{r}^{pk}b_{r}^{pk^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l(k+k^{\prime})}z_{lj}^{k+k^{\prime}}\frac{\lvert Z_{j}\rvert^{2r}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{2r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt(-1)^{e_{j}+g_{j}-k-k^{\prime}}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}+g_{j}-k-k^{\prime})}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})^{e_{j}+g_{j}-k-k^{\prime}}\kern 5.0pt.

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}+g_{j})}\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0,0}^{e_{j},g_{j}}c_{k}d_{k^{\prime}}\sum_{r=0}^{\min(pk,pk^{\prime})}a_{r}^{pk}b_{r}^{pk^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l(k+k^{\prime})}z_{lj}^{k+k^{\prime}}\frac{\lvert Z_{j}\rvert^{2r}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{2r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt(-1)^{e_{j}+g_{j}-k-k^{\prime}}(1\pm\delta^{\prime})^{e_{j}+g_{j}-k-k^{\prime}}\kern 5.0pt.

(71)

Noting that the term in the expectation is always non-negative, replacing the powers of -1 by 1 cannot decrease the
RHS
. Therefore, the
RHS
of Eqn. (
A.4
) is bounded above by

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+\delta)^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}+g_{j})}\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0,0}^{e_{j},g_{j}}c_{k}d_{k^{\prime}}\sum_{r=0}^{\min(pk,pk^{\prime})}a_{r}^{pk}b_{r}^{pk^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l(k+k^{\prime})}z_{lj}^{k+k^{\prime}}\frac{\lvert Z_{j}\rvert^{2r}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{2r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=(1+\delta)^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}+g_{j})}\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0,0}^{e_{j},g_{j}}c_{k}d_{k^{\prime}}\tau(k,k^{\prime}),

(72)

where,

\displaystyle\tau(k,k^{\prime})=\sum_{r=0}^{\min(pk,pk^{\prime})}a_{r}^{pk}b_{r}^{pk^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l(k+k^{\prime})}z_{lj}^{k+k^{\prime}}\frac{\lvert Z_{j}\rvert^{2r}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{2r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(73)

Repeating similar calculations, we obtain,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\overline{Y_{j}}^{g_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\geq(1-\delta^{\prime})^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}+g_{j})}\sum_{r=0}^{\min(e_{j},g_{j})}a_{r}^{pe_{j}}b_{r}^{pg_{j}}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l(e_{j}+g_{j})}z_{lj}^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\frac{\lvert Z_{j}\rvert^{2r}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{2r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=(1-\delta^{\prime})^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}+g_{j})}\tau(e_{j},g_{j})\kern 5.0pt.

(74)

We now consider the
\tau
function. Note that by definition,
\tau
is a symmetric function, that is,
\tau(k,k^{\prime})=\tau(k^{\prime},k)
. We wish to show that
\tau
is monotonic, that is, if
0\leq k\leq e_{j}
and
0\leq k^{\prime}\leq g_{j}
, then,
\tau(k,k^{\prime})\leq\tau(e_{j},g_{j})
. Assume that
0\leq k\leq e_{j}
and
0\leq k^{\prime}\leq g_{j}
.

Case 1.
Suppose
k+k^{\prime}=0
, or, equivalently,
k=0
and
k^{\prime}=0
. Then,
\tau(k,k^{\prime})=1
. Further,

\displaystyle\tau(e_{j},g_{j})

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{\min(pe_{j},pg_{j}^{\prime})}a_{r}^{pe_{j}}b_{r}^{pg_{j}}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l(e_{j}+g_{j})}z_{lj}^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\frac{\lvert Z_{j}\rvert^{2r}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{2r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{r=1}^{\min(pe_{j},pg_{j}^{\prime})}a_{r}^{pe_{j}}b_{r}^{pg_{j}}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l(e_{j}+g_{j})}z_{lj}^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\frac{\lvert Z_{j}\rvert^{2r}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{2r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1=\tau(k,k^{\prime})\kern 5.0pt.

Case 2.
Now suppose
k+k^{\prime}\geq 1
. We note that the function
a_{r}^{n}=\binom{n}{r}
is monotonic in the first argument, that is,
\binom{n+1}{r}\geq\binom{n}{r}
. This follows since, for
r=0
, these two terms are obviously equal to 1, and for
r>0
,

\displaystyle\binom{n+1}{r}=\prod_{j=0}^{r-1}\frac{n+1-j}{j}>\prod_{j=0}^{r-1}\frac{n-j}{j}=\binom{n}{r}\kern 5.0pt.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\tau(k,k^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{\min(pk,pk^{\prime})}\binom{pk}{r}\binom{pk^{\prime}}{r}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l(k+k^{\prime})}z_{lj}^{k+k^{\prime}}\frac{\lvert Z_{j}\rvert^{2r}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{2r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{r=0}^{\min(pe_{j},pg_{j})}\binom{pe_{j}}{r}\binom{pe_{j}}{r}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{l\textsf{~consist. with~}j}2^{l(e_{j}+g_{j})}z_{lj}^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\frac{\lvert Z_{j}\rvert^{2r}}{\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{2r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\tau(e_{j},g_{j})\kern 5.0pt.

The last step is obtained essentially by replacing each term in the expansion of
\tau(k,k^{\prime})
by a corresponding term that is a function of
e_{j}
and
g_{j}
and which is no smaller. First, the summation is extended from
\min(pk,pk^{\prime})
to
\min(pe_{j},pg_{j})
. Since the summation inside are all non-negative terms, the replacement cannot make it smaller. Secondly, the terms
\binom{pk}{r}
and
\binom{pk^{\prime}}{r}
are replaced by
\binom{pe_{j}}{r}
and
\binom{pg_{j}}{r}
respectively, which are each no smaller than its corresponding term. The term
2^{l(k+k^{\prime})}\leq 2^{l(e_{j}+g_{j})}
. Now
z_{lj}
is an indicator variable, and for
k+k^{\prime}\geq 1
,
z_{lj}=z_{lj}^{k+k^{\prime}}=z_{lj}^{e_{j}+g_{j}}
.

Thus, in all cases,
\tau
is a monotonic function, that is, for
0\leq k\leq e_{j}
and
\tau(k,k^{\prime})\leq\tau(e_{j},g_{j})
.

Continuing from Eqn. (
A.4
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{j}}(\overline{Y_{j}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1+\delta^{\prime})^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}+g_{j})}\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0,0}^{e_{j},g_{j}}c_{k}d_{k^{\prime}}\tau(k,k^{\prime})

\displaystyle=(1+\delta^{\prime})^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}+g_{j})}\tau(e_{j},g_{j})\sum_{k,k^{\prime}=0}^{e_{j},g_{j}}c_{k}d_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(1+\delta^{\prime})^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\lvert x_{j}\rvert^{p(e_{j}+g_{j})}\tau(e_{j},g_{j})2^{e_{j}+g_{j}}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2(1+\delta^{\prime})}{1-\delta^{\prime}}\right)^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\overline{Y_{j}}^{g_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

Let
G^{\prime}=\text{lmargin}(G_{0})\cup_{l=1}^{L}G_{l}
. Recall that
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{G}\wedge\textsc{nocollision}\wedge\textsc{accuest}
. Although, for the next lemma, it would suffice to condition on the event
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}=\mathcal{G}\wedge\textsc{nocollision}(G^{\prime})\wedge\textsc{accuest}(G^{\prime})
.

Lemma 40
(Re-statement of Lemma
11
.)

Let
B\geq Ln^{1-2/p}\epsilon^{-4/p}\log^{2/p}(1/\delta))
for a suitable constant
L
.
Then,
for integral
0\leq d_{1},d_{2}\leq\lceil\log(1/\delta)\rceil
, we have,

\mathbf{E}\biggl[\left(\sum_{i\in G^{\prime}}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in G^{\prime}}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\overline{\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]})\right)^{d_{2}}\bigg|\mathcal{H}\biggr]\leq\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{20}\right)^{d_{1}+d_{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof:
For
d_{1}+d_{2}=0
, that is,
d_{1}=0
and
d_{2}=0
, the statement of the lemma is vacuously true.
If
d_{1}+d_{2}=1
, then, say
d_{1}=1
, then,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{i\in S}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\right])\right]=\sum_{i\in S}\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p}n^{-\Omega(1)}=F_{p}n^{-\Omega(1)}

implying the statement of the Lemma.

We therefore assume that
d_{1}+d_{2}>1
.
Using the notation that
S_{1}=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r}\}
and
S_{2}=\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{t}\}
, we can rewrite the summation by using three subsets:
T_{1}=S_{1}\cap S_{2}
,
T_{2}=S_{1}\setminus S_{2}
and
T_{3}=S_{2}\setminus S_{1}
, where,
\lvert T_{1}\rvert=r,\lvert T_{2}\rvert=s
and
\lvert T_{3}\rvert=t
. Without loss of generality, let
d_{1}\leq d_{2}
—the other case is symmetric.

Then, the summation may be written as

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\left(\sum_{i\in S}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in S}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\overline{\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]})\right)^{d_{2}}\right|\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{d_{1}}\sum_{s=\max(0,1-r)}^{d_{1}-r}\sum_{t=\max(0,1-r)}^{d_{2}-r}\sum_{a=0}^{s}\sum_{b=0}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r+s}=d_{1}\\
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}\geq 1\\
\bigl\lvert\{j\in[s]:e_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=a\\
A=\{j\in[s]:e_{r+j}=1\}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{r+t}=d_{2}\\
\text{ $g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1$}\\
\bigl\lvert\{j:g_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=b\\
B=\{k\in[t]:g_{r+k}=1\}\end{subarray}}\binom{d_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}}

\displaystyle~~~\binom{d_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{r+t}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r},j_{1},\ldots,j_{s},k_{1},\ldots,k_{t}\}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{v\in A}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\prod_{w\in B}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt\prod_{u=1}^{r}(Y_{i_{u}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{u}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{u}}(\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}\right])^{g_{u}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\left.\left.\prod_{v\in[s]\setminus A}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]\setminus B}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{r+w}}\right|\mathcal{H}\right]

Let
\gamma=n^{-\Omega(1)}
.
Using Lemma
33
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{v\in A}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\prod_{w\in B}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\prod_{u=1}^{r}(Y_{i_{u}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{u}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{u}}(\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{u}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\prod_{v\in[s]\setminus A}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]\setminus B}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{r+w}}\right|\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{a+b}\prod_{v\in A}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w\in B}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{u=1}^{r}(Y_{i_{u}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{u}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{u}}(\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{u}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\left.\left.\prod_{v\in[s]\setminus A}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]\setminus B}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{r+w}}\right|\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\gamma^{a+b}\prod_{v\in A}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w\in B}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{u=1}^{r}\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{i_{u}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{u}}\right])^{e_{u}}(\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}\right])^{g_{u}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\prod_{v\in[s]\setminus A}\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{r+v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\prod_{w\in[t]\setminus B}\mathbf{E}\left[(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{r+w}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(76)

We will now use Lemma
39
, which states that, for
e_{j},g_{j}\geq 0
,

\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{j}}(\overline{Y_{j}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{j}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq\left(2\left(\frac{1+\delta^{\prime}}{1-\delta^{\prime}}\right)\right)^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j}^{e_{j}}\overline{Y_{j}}^{g_{j}}\right]

where,
\delta^{\prime}=n^{-\Omega(1)}
.

Note that for
\delta^{\prime}=n^{-\Omega(1)}
, where, the constant term in
\Omega(1)
can be made as large as needed by choosing the parameter of width of hash tables appropriately, and since,
e_{j}+g_{j}\leq\log(1/\delta)\leq n^{2/p}
, it follows that
\left(\frac{1+\delta^{\prime}}{1-\delta^{\prime}}\right)^{e_{j}+g_{j}}\leq 1+\delta^{\prime\prime}
, where,
\delta^{\prime\prime}=n^{-\Omega(1)}
for a different constant in
\Omega(1)
. Also,
(1+\delta^{\prime\prime})^{\lceil\log(1/\delta)\rceil}\leq 2
, which we will use below.

Substituting in Eqn. (
A.4
), we have,
