\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{v\in A}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\prod_{w\in B}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\prod_{u=1}^{r}(Y_{i_{u}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{u}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{u}}(\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{u}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\prod_{v\in[s]\setminus A}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]\setminus B}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{r+w}}\right|\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{a+b}\prod_{v\in A}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w\in B}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}\cdot(2)\cdot 2^{\sum_{u=1}^{r}(e_{u}+g_{u})+\sum_{v\in[s]}e_{r+v}+\sum_{w\in[t]}g_{r+w}}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\prod_{u=1}^{r}\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{u}}^{e_{u}}\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}^{g_{u}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\prod_{v\in[s]\setminus A}\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}^{e_{r+v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\prod_{w\in[t]\setminus B}\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}^{g_{r+w}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Now, the sum of the exponents in the power of 2 in the above expression is
d_{1}+d_{2}-a-b
. Thus, the above equation becomes,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{v\in A}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\prod_{w\in B}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\prod_{u=1}^{r}(Y_{i_{u}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{u}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{u}}(\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{u}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\prod_{v\in[s]\setminus A}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]\setminus B}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{r+w}}|\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{a+b}2^{d_{1}+d_{2}-a-b+1}\prod_{v\in A}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w\in B}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{u=1}^{r}\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{u}}^{e_{u}}\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}^{g_{u}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\prod_{v\in[s]\setminus A}\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}^{e_{r+v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 216.81pt\prod_{w\in[t]\setminus B}\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}^{g_{r+w}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\gamma^{a+b}2^{d_{1}+d_{2}-a-b+1}\prod_{v\in A}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w\in B}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{u=1}^{r}Y_{i_{u}}^{e_{u}}\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}^{g_{u}}\prod_{v\in[s]\setminus A}Y_{j_{v}}^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]\setminus B}\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}^{g_{r+w}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right],

(77)

where we have used the fact that
\sum_{u=1}^{r}(e_{u}+g_{u})+\sum_{v=1}^{s}e_{r+v}+\sum_{w=1}^{t}g_{r+w}=d_{1}+d_{2}
.

By Lemma
35
, we have, for
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r},g_{1},\ldots,g_{r}\geq 1
and
e_{r+1},\ldots,e_{r+t}\geq 2
and
g_{r+1},\ldots,g_{r+t}\geq 2
, that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{u=1}^{r}Y_{i_{u}}^{e_{u}}\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}^{g_{u}}\prod_{v\in[s]}Y_{j_{v}}^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]}\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}^{g_{r+v}}|\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{\sum_{u\in[r]}(e_{u}+g_{u})/8}\prod_{u=1}^{r}\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{v=1}^{s}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w=1}^{t}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\left(\frac{4F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)\left(\sum_{u=1}^{r}(e_{u}+g_{u}-1)+\sum_{v\in[s]}(e_{r+v}-1)+\sum_{w\in[t]}(e_{r+w}-1)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{(d_{1}+d_{2})/8}\prod_{u=1}^{r}\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{v\in[s]}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w\in[t]}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}\left(\frac{4F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)(d_{1}+d_{2}-r-s-t-a-b)}

(78)

using the fact that
\sum_{u=1}^{r}(e_{u}+g_{u})+\sum_{v=1}^{s}e_{r+v}+\sum_{w=1}^{t}g_{r+w}=d_{1}+d_{2}
.

Substituting Eqn (
A.4
) in Eqn. (
A.4
) we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{v\in A}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\prod_{w\in B}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\prod_{u=1}^{r}(Y_{i_{u}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{u}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{u}}(\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{u}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{u}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\prod_{v\in[s]\setminus A}(Y_{j_{v}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{v}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{r+v}}\prod_{w\in[t]\setminus B}(\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{k_{w}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{r+w}}\right|\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\gamma^{a+b}2^{d_{1}+d_{2}}e^{(d_{1}+d_{2})/8}\prod_{u=1}^{r}\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{v\in[s]}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w\in[t]}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}\left(\frac{4F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)(d_{1}+d_{2}-r-s-t-a-b)}

(79)

Substituting Eqn. (
A.4
) in Eqn. (
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\left(\sum_{i\in S}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in S}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\overline{\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]})\right)^{d_{2}}\right|\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\cdot 2^{d_{1}+d_{2}}e^{(d_{1}+d_{2})/8}\sum_{r=0}^{d_{1}}\sum_{s=\max(0,1-r)}^{d_{1}-r}\sum_{t=\max(0,1-r)}^{d_{2}-r}\sum_{a=0}^{s}\sum_{b=0}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r+s}=d_{1}\\
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}\geq 1\\
\bigl\lvert\{j\in[s]:e_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=a\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{r+t}=d_{2}\\
\text{ $g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1$}\\
\bigl\lvert\{j:g_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=b\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle~~\binom{d_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}}\binom{d_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{r+t}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r},j_{1},\ldots j_{s},k_{1},\ldots,k_{t}\}\end{subarray}}\gamma^{a+b}\prod_{u=1}^{r}\lvert x_{i_{u}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{v\in[s]}\lvert x_{j_{v}}\rvert^{p}\prod_{w\in[t]}\lvert x_{k_{w}}\rvert^{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\left(\frac{4F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)(d_{1}+d_{2}-r-s-t-a-b)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\frac{8e^{1/(4p)}F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)(d_{1}+d_{2})}\sum_{r=0}^{d_{1}}\sum_{s=\max(0,1-r)}^{\lfloor(d_{1}-r)\rfloor}\sum_{t=\max(0,1-r)}^{\lfloor(d_{2}-r)\rfloor}\sum_{a=0}^{s}\sum_{b=0}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r+s}=d_{1}\\
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}\geq 1\\
\bigl\lvert\{j\in[s]:e_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=a\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{r+t}=d_{2}\\
\text{ $g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1$}\\
\bigl\lvert\{j:g_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=b\end{subarray}}\binom{d_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}}\binom{d_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{r+t}}\frac{\gamma^{a+b}F_{p}^{r+s+t}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)(r+s+t+a+b)}(r+s+t)!}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\frac{8e^{1/(4p)}F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)(d_{1}+d_{2})}\sum_{r=0}^{d_{1}}\sum_{s=\max(0,1-r)}^{d_{1}-r}\sum_{t=\max(0,1-r)}^{d_{2}-r}\frac{F_{p}^{r+s+t}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)(r+s+t)}(r+s+t)!}

\displaystyle\sum_{a=0}^{s}\sum_{b=0}^{t}\frac{\gamma^{a+b}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)(a+b)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r+s}=d_{1}\\
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}\geq 1\\
\bigl\lvert\{j\in[s]:e_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=a\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{r+t}=d_{2}\\
\text{ $g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1$}\\
\bigl\lvert\{j:g_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=b\end{subarray}}\binom{d_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}}\binom{d_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{r+t}}1

(80)

Fix
r,s,t,a
and
b
as per the constraints, and consider the inner summation. Given the vectors
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}
and
g_{1},\ldots,g_{r+t}
, define the vectors
e^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,e^{\prime}_{r+s}
and
g^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,g^{\prime}_{r+s}
as follows.
e^{\prime}_{j}=e_{j}-1
, for
j\in[r]
,
e^{\prime}_{r+j}=e_{r+j}-2
, for
j\in[t]
and
e_{r+j}\neq 1
, and
e^{\prime}_{r+j}=0
, if
e_{r+j}=1
. Similarly, the
r+t
-dimensional vector
g^{\prime}
is defined. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r+s}=d_{1}\\
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}\geq 1\\
\bigl\lvert\{j\in[s]:e_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=a\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{r+t}=d_{2}\\
\text{ $g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1$}\\
\bigl\lvert\{j:g_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=b\end{subarray}}\binom{d_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}}\binom{d_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{r+t}}1

\displaystyle=\Biggl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r+s}=d_{1}\\
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}\geq 1\\
\bigl\lvert\{j\in[s]:e_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=a\end{subarray}}\binom{d_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}}1\Biggr)\Biggl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{r+t}=d_{1}\\
\text{ $g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1$}\\
\bigl\lvert\{j:g_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=b\end{subarray}}\binom{d_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{r+t}}1\Biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\Biggl(d_{1}^{r+a+2(s-a)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e^{\prime}_{1}+\ldots e^{\prime}_{r+s}=d-(r+2s-a)\\
\bigl\lvert\{j:e^{\prime}_{r+j}=0\}\bigr\rvert=a\end{subarray}}\binom{d_{1}-(r+2s-a)}{e^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,e^{\prime}_{r+s}}\Biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\Biggl(d_{2}^{r+b+2(t-b)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g^{\prime}_{1}+\ldots g^{\prime}_{r+t}=d_{2}-(r+2t-b)\\
\bigl\lvert\{j:g^{\prime}_{r+j}=0\}\bigr\rvert=b\end{subarray}}\binom{d_{2}-(r+2t-b)}{g^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,g^{\prime}_{r+t}}\Biggr)

(81)

Now,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e^{\prime}_{1}+\ldots e^{\prime}_{r+s}=d_{1}-(r+2s-a)\\
\bigl\lvert\{j:e^{\prime}_{r+j}=0\}\bigr\rvert=a\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\binom{d_{1}-(r+2s-a)}{e^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,e^{\prime}_{r+s}}

\displaystyle=\binom{r+s}{a}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f_{1}+\ldots,f_{r+s-a}=d_{1}-(r+2s-a)\end{subarray}}\binom{d_{1}-(r+2s-a)}{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r+s-a}}

\displaystyle=\binom{r+s}{a}(r+s-a)^{d_{1}-(r+2s-a)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(r+s)^{d_{1}-(r+2s-a)+a}}{a!}

Similarly,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g^{\prime}_{1}+\ldots g^{\prime}_{r+t}=d_{2}-(r+2t-b)\\
\bigl\lvert\{j:g^{\prime}_{r+j}=0\}\bigr\rvert=b\end{subarray}}\binom{d_{2}-(r+2t-b)}{g^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,g^{\prime}_{r+t}}\leq\frac{(r+t)^{d_{2}-(r+2t-b)+b}}{b!}\kern 5.0pt.

Substituting in (
A.4
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r+s}=d_{1}\\
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}\geq 1\\
\bigl\lvert\{j\in[s]:e_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=a\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{r+t}=d_{2}\\
\text{ $g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1$}\\
\bigl\lvert\{j:g_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=b\end{subarray}}\binom{d_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}}\binom{d_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{r+t}}1

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{a!b!}\right)d_{1}^{r+2s-a}d_{2}^{r+2t-b}(r+s)^{d_{1}-r-2s+2a}(r+t)^{d_{2}-r-2t+2b}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{a=0}^{s}\sum_{b=0}^{t}\frac{\gamma^{a+b}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)(a+b)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.68121pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r+s}=d\\
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}\geq 1\\
\bigl\lvert\{j\in[s]:e_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=a\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{r+t}=d\\
\text{ $g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1$}\\
\bigl\lvert\{j:g_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=b\end{subarray}}\binom{d}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}}\binom{d}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{r+t}}1

\displaystyle\leq d_{1}^{r+2s}d_{2}^{r+2t}(r+s)^{d_{1}-r-2s}(r+t)^{d_{2}-r-2t}\sum_{a=0}^{s}\sum_{b=0}^{t}\left(\frac{\gamma}{(4F_{2}/B)^{p/2}}\right)^{a+b}

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt\left(\frac{(r+s)^{2a}(r+t)^{2b}}{d_{1}^{a}d_{2}^{b}}\right)

\displaystyle=d_{1}^{r+2s}d_{2}^{r+2t}(r+s)^{d_{1}-r-2s}(r+t)^{d_{2}-r-2t}\left(\sum_{a=0}^{s}\left(\frac{\gamma(r+s)^{2}}{d_{1}(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)}}\right)^{a}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt\left(\sum_{b=0}^{t}\left(\frac{\gamma(r+s)^{2}}{d_{2}(4F_{2}/B)^{p/2}}\right)^{b}\right)

\displaystyle\leq d_{1}^{r+2s}d_{2}^{r+2t}(r+s)^{d_{1}-r-2s}(r+t)^{d_{2}-r-2t}\left(1+\gamma^{\prime}\right)\left(1+\gamma^{\prime}\right)

(82)

\displaystyle\leq 2d_{1}^{r+2s}d_{2}^{r+2t}(r+s)^{d_{1}-r-2s}(r+t)^{d_{2}-r-2t}\kern 5.0pt.

\displaystyle\leq 2d_{2}^{2r+2s+2t}(r+s+t)^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2r-2s-2t}

(83)

Eqn. (
82
) is obtained from the previous step as follows. Let

\gamma^{\prime}=2\max\left(\frac{\gamma(r+s)^{2}}{d_{1}(4F_{2}/B)^{p/2}},\frac{\gamma(r+s)^{2}}{d_{2}(4F_{2}/B)^{p/2}}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Then,
\gamma^{\prime}=O(\gamma(d_{1}+d_{2})^{2})=n^{-\Omega(1)}
and

\sum_{a=0}^{s}\left(\frac{\gamma(r+s)^{2}}{d_{1}(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)}}\right)^{a}\leq 1+\gamma^{\prime},\text{ and }\sum_{b=0}^{s}\left(\frac{\gamma(r+s)^{2}}{d_{2}(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)}}\right)^{b}\leq 1+\gamma^{\prime}\kern 5.0pt.

The last step, that is, Eqn. (
83
) is obtained from its previous equation as follows. (1)
\displaystyle(r+s)^{d_{1}-r-2s}(r+t)^{d_{2}-r-2s}\leq(r+s+t)^{d_{1}-r-2s}(r+s+t)^{d_{2}-r-2t}=(r+s+t)^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2r-2s-2t}
, and (2) by assumption,
d_{1}\leq d_{2}
.

Substituting Eqn. (
83
) in Eqn. (
A.4
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\left(\sum_{i\in S}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in S}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\overline{\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]})\right)^{d_{2}}\right|\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{d_{1}+d_{2}+2}e^{(d_{1}+d_{2})/8}\left(\frac{4F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)(d_{1}+d_{2})}\sum_{r=0}^{d_{1}}\sum_{s=\max(0,1-r)}^{d_{1}-r}\sum_{t=\max(0,1-r)}^{d_{2}-r}\frac{F_{p}^{r+s+t}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)(r+s+t)}(r+s+t)!}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\sum_{a=0}^{s}\sum_{b=0}^{t}\frac{\gamma^{a+b}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)(a+b)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{r+s}=d_{1}\\
e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}\geq 1\\
\bigl\lvert\{j\in[s]:e_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=a\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{r+t}=d_{2}\\
\text{ $g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1$}\\
\bigl\lvert\{j:g_{r+j}=1\}\bigr\rvert=b\end{subarray}}\binom{d_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{r+s}}\binom{d_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{r+t}}1

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2}\left(\frac{8e^{1/(4p)}F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)(d_{1}+d_{2})}\sum_{r=0}^{d_{1}}\sum_{s=\max(0,1-r)}^{d_{1}-r}\sum_{t=\max(0,1-r)}^{d_{2}-r}\frac{F_{p}^{r+s+t}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)(r+s+t)}(r+s+t)!}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135ptd_{2}^{2r+2s+2t}(r+s+t)^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2r-2s-2t}\kern 5.0pt.

(84)

Letting
u=r+s+t
in Eqn. (
84
), we obtain,

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2}\left(\frac{8e^{1/(4p)}F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)(d_{1}+d_{2})}\sum_{u=1}^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}\left(\frac{F_{p}^{u}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)u}u!}\right)d_{2}^{2u}u^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2u}

(85)

since,
2r+2s+2t\leq d_{1}+d_{2}
and therefore,
u=r+s+t\leq\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor
.

Taking ratio of the
u+1
st term to the
u
th term in the summation in Eqn. (
85
), we have,

\displaystyle\frac{F_{p}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)}}\cdot\frac{d_{2}^{2}}{(u+1)^{3}}\cdot(1+1/u)^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2u}\geq\frac{F_{p}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)}}\cdot\frac{d_{2}^{2}}{(u+1)^{3}}

Since,
F_{2}\leq n^{1-2/p}F_{p}^{2/p}
and
B\geq Kn^{1-2/p}d_{2}^{2/p}
, it follows that

\frac{F_{p}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)}}\geq(K/4)^{p/2}d_{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Therefore, the ratio of
u+1
st term to the
u
th term is at least

\displaystyle\frac{F_{p}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{(p/2)}}\cdot\frac{d_{2}^{2}}{(u+1)^{3}}\geq\frac{(K/4)^{p/2}d_{2}^{3}}{(u+1)^{3}}\geq 2

for
K\geq 8
since,
d_{2}\geq\lfloor d_{1}+d_{2}\rfloor/2\geq u+1
.

Therefore, the series
\displaystyle\sum_{u=1}^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}\left(\frac{F_{p}^{u}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{u}u!}\right)d_{2}^{2u}u^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2u}
is bounded above by twice the last term, that is,

\displaystyle\sum_{u=1}^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}\left(\frac{F_{p}^{u}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{u}u!}\right)d_{2}^{2u}u^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2u}

\displaystyle\leq(2)\left(\frac{F_{p}^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor!)}\right)d_{2}^{2\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt\left(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}

(86)

Substituting in Eqn. (
85
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\left(\sum_{i\in S}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in S}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\overline{\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]})\right)^{d_{2}}\right|\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{3}e^{(d_{1}+d_{2})/8}\left(\frac{8F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)(d_{1}+d_{2})}\left(\frac{F_{p}^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}}{(4F_{2}/B)^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor!)}\right)d_{2}^{2\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\left(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{3}e^{(d_{1}+d_{2})/8}\left(\frac{8F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)\left((d_{1}+d_{2})-\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)}F_{p}^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\frac{d_{2}^{2\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}}{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor!}\left(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}

(87)

By Stirling’s approximation, that is,
\displaystyle n!>(2\pi n)^{1/2}\left(\frac{n}{e}\right)^{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor!>(2\pi\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor))^{1/2}\left(\frac{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}{e}\right)^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}
.

Since,
d_{1}+d_{2}>1
, and we have assumed that
d_{2}\geq d_{1}
,

d_{2}\leq 3\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{d_{2}^{2\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}}{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor!}

\displaystyle\leq(3\sqrt{e})^{d_{1}+d_{2}}\left(\frac{\left(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)^{2\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\left(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor+1/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-1/2}(3\sqrt{e})^{d_{1}+d_{2}}\left(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor-1/2}

(88)

The term
\left(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}
in Eqn. (
A.4
) is 1 if
d_{1}+d_{2}
is odd and is
\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor
if
d_{1}+d_{2}
is even. We write this as the indicator variable
\displaystyle\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor^{\textbf{1}_{d_{1}+d_{2}\text{ odd }}}
.

Using this and substituting Eqn. (
88
) into Eqn. (
A.4
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left.\left(\sum_{i\in S}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in S}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\overline{\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]})\right)^{d_{2}}\right|\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{3}e^{(d_{1}+d_{2})/8}\left(\frac{8F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)\left((d_{1}+d_{2})-\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)}F_{p}^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\frac{d_{2}^{2\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}}{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor!}\left(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)^{d_{1}+d_{2}-2\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{3}e^{(d_{1}+d_{2})/8}(2\pi)^{-1/2}(3\sqrt{e})^{d_{1}+d_{2}}\left(\frac{8F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)\lceil(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rceil}F_{p}^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\left(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor-1/2+\textbf{1}_{d_{1}+d_{2}\text{ odd}}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{3}\left(3\cdot e\right)^{d_{1}+d_{2}}\left(\frac{8F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)\lceil(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rceil}F_{p}^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}\left(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)^{(d_{1}+d_{2})/2}

(89)

Now,
B\geq Ln^{1-2/p}\epsilon^{-4/p}\log^{2/p}(1/\delta)
, for an appropriate constant
L\geq(2^{3})^{2/p}\cdot\left((8\cdot 20)(3e)\right)^{4/p}
. Using
F_{2}\leq n^{1-2/p}F_{p}^{2/p}
, we have,

\displaystyle\left(2^{6/p}(3e)^{2}\cdot\frac{8F_{2}}{B}\right)^{p/2}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}F_{p}}{400\lceil\log(1/\delta)\rceil}

Therefore, assuming
d_{1}+d_{2}\geq 1
, Eqn. (
89
) is bounded above by

\displaystyle 2^{3}\left(3e\right)^{d_{1}+d_{2}}\left(\frac{8F_{2}}{B}\right)^{(p/2)\lceil(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rceil}F_{p}^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}\left(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)^{(d_{1}+d_{2})/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\right)^{(d_{1}+d_{2})/2}\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}F_{p}}{400\lceil\log(1/\delta)\rceil}\right)^{\lceil(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rceil}F_{p}^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{20}\right)^{d_{1}+d_{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

Here, the last step uses that, (i)
\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor\leq\lceil\log(1/\delta)\rceil
, (ii)
\epsilon^{2\lceil(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rceil}\leq\epsilon^{d_{1}+d_{2}}
, and

(iii)
F_{p}^{\lfloor(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rfloor+\lceil(d_{1}+d_{2})/2\rceil}=F_{p}^{d_{1}+d_{2}}
.

A.5
Analysis of contribution to
d
th moment from items in
\text{mid}(G_{0})

Recall that
\mathcal{H}
denotes the event
\mathcal{G}\wedge\textsc{nocollision}\wedge\textsc{goodest}
.

Lemma 41
(Re-stated (expanded) version of Lemma
12
.)

Let
1\leq e,g\leq\lceil\log(1/\delta)\rceil
and
l\in\text{mid}(G_{0})
. Assume
C\geq 72p^{2}B
and
\lvert x_{l}\rvert\geq\left(\frac{F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{B}\right)^{1/2}
. Let the family
\{\omega_{lr}(i)\}_{i\in[n]}
be
O(k)
-wise independent where,
k\geq O(\log(1/\delta)+\log n)
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\left(\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{g}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]
is real and

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\left(\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{g}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq\left(\frac{7p^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\rho C\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}}\right)^{(e+g)/2}\kern 5.0pt.

It follows that,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(Y_{l}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{l}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\right)^{e}\left(\overline{Y_{l}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{l}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\right)^{g}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq\left(\frac{8p^{2}\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2p-2}F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\rho C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof:

Let
c_{r}=\binom{p}{r}
, for
r\geq 0
. Since,
\frac{\lvert Z_{l}\rvert}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\leq 1/8
, the binomial series expansion of
\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}
holds as the power series
\sum_{r\geq 0}c_{r}\left(\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{r}
. For the first part of the proof, we will assume full independence of the family
\{\omega_{lr}(i)\}_{i\in[n]}
, for
l=0
and
r\in[2s]
so that we can use the above binomial expansion.

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\left(\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{g}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{r\geq 1}c_{r}\left(\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{r}\right)^{e}\left(\sum_{s\geq 1}c_{s}\left(\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{s}\right)^{g}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\frac{c_{1}Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{e}\left(\frac{c_{1}\overline{Z_{l}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{g}\left(\sum_{r\geq 1}\frac{c_{r}}{c_{1}}\left(\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{r-1}\right)^{e}\left(\sum_{s\geq 1}\frac{c_{s}}{c_{1}}\left(\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{s-1}\right)^{g}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{a_{1}+\ldots+a_{k}+\ldots=e}\sum_{b_{1}+\ldots+b_{k}+\ldots=g}c_{1}^{e+g}\prod_{r\geq 1}\left(\frac{c_{r}}{c_{1}}\right)^{a_{r}}\prod_{s\geq 1}\left(\frac{c_{s}}{c_{1}}\right)^{b_{s}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\mathbf{E}\left[\cfrac{Z_{l}^{e+\sum_{r\geq 1}(r-1)a_{r}}\overline{Z_{l}}^{g+\sum_{s\geq 1}(s-1)b_{s}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{\sum_{r\geq 1}(r-1)a_{r}+\sum_{s\geq 1}(s-1)b_{r}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

(90)

Consider the term
\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\frac{Z_{l}^{e+\sum_{r\geq 1}(r-1)a_{r}}\overline{Z_{l}}^{g+\sum_{s\geq 1}(s-1)b_{s}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{\sum_{r\geq 1}(r-1)a_{r}+\sum_{s\geq 1}(s-1)b_{r}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]
. Subject to the constraint that
a_{1}+\ldots+a_{k}+\ldots=e
and
b_{1}+\ldots+b_{k}+\ldots=g
, we have,
e+\sum_{r\geq 1}(r-1)a_{r}=\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r}
and
g+\sum_{s\geq 1}(s-1)b_{s}=\sum_{s\geq 1}sb_{s}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{l}^{e+\sum_{r\geq 1}(r-1)a_{r}}\overline{Z_{l}}^{g+\sum_{s\geq 1}(s-1)b_{s}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{l}^{\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r}}\overline{Z_{l}}^{\sum_{s\geq 1}sb_{s}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

This expectation is 0 if
\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r}\neq\sum_{s\geq 1}sb_{s}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\left(\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{g}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{a_{1}+\ldots+a_{k}+\ldots=e}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1}+\ldots+b_{k}+\ldots=g\\
\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r}=\sum_{s\geq 1}sb_{s}\end{subarray}}c_{1}^{e+g}\prod_{r\geq 1}\left(\frac{c_{r}}{c_{1}}\right)^{a_{r}}\prod_{s\geq 1}\left(\frac{c_{s}}{c_{1}}\right)^{b_{s}}\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[(Z\overline{Z})^{\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r}}}

(91)

From Eqn. (
A.5
), it follows that the
RHS
is a real number, and therefore the expectation in the
LHS
is real.

For non-zero expectation, assuming
\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r}=\sum_{s\geq 1}sb_{s}
, we have,

\displaystyle 0\leq\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[(Z_{l}\overline{Z_{l}})^{\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[(Z_{l}\overline{Z_{l}})^{(e+g)/2}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{e+g}}\left(\frac{\lvert Z_{l}\rvert}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{2(\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r})-(e+g)}

\displaystyle\leq\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{-(e+g)}\left(\frac{(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{2(\rho/9)C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}\varrho^{2(\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r})-(e+g)}

where,
\varrho=(B/C)^{1/2}
. Note that conditional on
goodest
,
\lvert Z_{l}\rvert/\lvert x_{l}\rvert\leq\rho
.

Substituting, and noting that
c_{1}=\binom{p}{1}=p
, the sum in Eqn. (
90
) is bounded above as follows.

\displaystyle\sum_{a_{1}+\ldots+a_{k}+\ldots=e}\sum_{b_{1}+\ldots+b_{k}+\ldots=g}c_{1}^{e+g}\prod_{r\geq 1}\left(\frac{c_{r}}{c_{1}}\right)^{a_{r}}\prod_{s\geq 1}\left(\frac{c_{s}}{c_{1}}\right)^{b_{s}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\mathbf{E}\left[\cfrac{Z_{l}^{e+\sum_{r\geq 1}(r-1)a_{r}}\overline{Z_{l}}^{g+\sum_{s\geq 1}(s-1)b_{s}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{\sum_{r\geq 1}(r-1)a_{r}+\sum_{s\geq 1}(s-1)b_{r}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{-(e+g)}\left(\frac{c_{1}^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{2(\rho/9)C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\sum_{a_{1}+\ldots+a_{k}+\ldots=e}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1}+\ldots+b_{k}+\ldots=g\\
\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r}=\sum_{s\geq 1}sb_{s}\end{subarray}}\prod_{r\geq 1}\left(\frac{\lvert c_{r}\rvert}{c_{1}}\right)^{a_{r}}\prod_{s\geq 1}\left(\frac{\lvert c_{s}\rvert}{c_{1}}\right)^{b_{s}}\varrho^{2(\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r})-(e+g)}

\displaystyle\leq\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{-(e+g)}\left(\frac{c_{1}^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{2(\rho/9)C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\sum_{a_{1}+\ldots+a_{k}+\ldots=e}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1}+\ldots+b_{k}+\ldots=g\end{subarray}}\prod_{r\geq 1}\left(\frac{\lvert c_{r}\rvert}{c_{1}}\right)^{a_{r}}\prod_{s\geq 1}\left(\frac{\lvert c_{s}\rvert}{c_{1}}\right)^{b_{s}}\varrho^{\left(\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r}+\sum_{s\geq 1}sb_{s}\right)-(e+g)}

(92)

Using,
\sum_{r\geq 1}a_{r}=e
and
\sum_{s\geq 1}b_{s}=g
, we have,
\left(\sum_{r\geq 1}ra_{r}+\sum_{s\geq 1}sb_{s}\right)-(e+g)=\sum_{r\geq 1}(r-1)a_{r}+\sum_{s\geq 1}(s-1)b_{s}
. Therefore, Eqn. (
92
) equals

\displaystyle\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{-(e+g)}\left(\frac{c_{1}^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{2(\rho/9)C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}\left(\sum_{r\geq 1}\frac{\lvert c_{r}\rvert}{c_{1}}\varrho^{r-1}\right)^{e}\left(\sum_{s\geq 1}\frac{\lvert c_{s}\rvert}{c_{1}}\varrho^{s-1}\right)^{g}

(93)

The ratio of the
(r+1)
th term to the
r
th term in the summation
\sum_{r\geq 1}\frac{\lvert c_{r}\rvert}{c_{1}}\varrho^{r-1}
, for
r=1,2,\ldots,k-1
is

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{c_{r+1}}{c_{r}}\right\rvert\varrho=\frac{\lvert p-r\rvert\varrho}{r+1}\leq\frac{1}{8}

since,
\varrho=(B/C)^{1/2}\leq 1/(8p)
. Therefore,

\sum_{r\geq 1}\frac{\lvert c_{r}\rvert}{c_{1}}\varrho^{r-1}\leq\sum_{r\geq 1}(1/8)^{r-1}\leq(9/8)
.

The summation in Eqn. (
93
) is therefore bounded above by (since,
c_{1}=p
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\left(\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{g}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{-(e+g)}\left(\frac{((9/8)p)^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{2(\rho/9)C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{6p^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}\rho C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}\kern 5.0pt.

(94)

Proceeding similarly, we show that for any
k\geq 1
,
\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{r=1}^{k}c_{r}\frac{Z_{l}^{r}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{r}}\right)^{e}\left(\sum_{s=1}^{k}c_{s}\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}^{s}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{r}}\right)^{g}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]
is real and

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{r=1}^{k}c_{r}\frac{Z_{l}^{r}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{r}}\right)^{e}\left(\sum_{s=1}^{k}c_{s}\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}^{s}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{r}}\right)^{g}\right]\leq\left(\frac{6p^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}\rho C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}\kern 5.0pt.

(95)

Using
k
-wise independence.
Let
k=\Omega(\log n)
be a parameter to be determined and assume that the family
\{\omega_{lr}(i)\}_{i\in[n]}
is at least
k
-wise independent. With probability
1-n^{-\Omega(1)}
,
\lvert Z_{l}\rvert\leq\left(\frac{F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{C}\right)^{1/2}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\lvert Z_{l}\rvert}{x_{l}}\leq\left(\frac{F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}C}\right)^{1/2}
.
By Taylor’s series expansion up to
k
terms, we have,
\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}=\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}c_{r}\frac{Z_{l}^{r}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{r}}+\gamma_{k}
, where,
\displaystyle\gamma_{k}=c_{k}\frac{Z_{l}^{{}^{\prime}k}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{k}}
and
\lvert Z^{\prime}_{l}\rvert\leq\lvert Z_{l}\rvert
. By the above discussion, we have,
\displaystyle\lvert\gamma_{k}\rvert=\frac{\lvert c_{k}Z_{l}^{{}^{\prime}k}\rvert}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{k}}\leq\left\lvert\binom{p}{k}\right\rvert\left(\frac{F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}C}\right)^{k/2}=\zeta
(say).

In the remainder of the proof,
all expectations are conditional on

\mathcal{H}
.

Let
\alpha_{k}
denote the sum of the first
k-1
terms in the Taylor series expansion of
\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}
except for the zeroth term, that is,

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}=\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}c_{r}\frac{Z_{l}^{r}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{r}}\kern 5.0pt.

Hence,
\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1=\alpha_{k}+\gamma_{k}
. Let
\beta_{k}=\overline{\alpha_{k}}
and so that
\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1=\beta_{k}+\overline{\gamma_{k}}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\left(\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{g}\right]=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\alpha_{k}+\gamma_{k}\right)^{e}\left(\beta_{k}+\overline{\gamma_{k}}\right)^{g}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Let
d_{r}=\binom{e}{r}
, for
r=0,1,\ldots,e
and
h_{s}=\binom{g}{s}
, for
s=0,1,\ldots,g
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\alpha_{k}+\gamma_{k}\right)^{e}\left(\beta_{k}+\overline{\gamma_{k}}\right)^{g}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{r=0}^{e}d_{r}\alpha_{k}^{e-r}\gamma_{k}^{r}\right)\left(\sum_{s=0}^{g}h_{s}\beta_{k}^{g-s}\overline{\gamma_{k}}^{s}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha_{k}^{e}\beta_{k}^{g}\right]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r=0\ldots e,s=0\ldots g\\
r+s\geq 1\end{subarray}}d_{r}h_{s}\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha_{k}^{e-r}\beta_{k}^{g-s}\gamma_{k}^{r}\overline{\gamma_{k}}^{s}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(96)

By Eqn. (
A.5
), the
LHS
of Eqn. (
A.5
) is non-negative and real.

By Eqn. (
95
),
\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha_{k}^{e}\beta_{k}^{g}\right]
is non-negative, real and
\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha_{k}^{e}\beta_{k}^{g}\right]\leq\left(\frac{6p^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}\rho C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}
.

We now consider the term
\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha_{k}^{e-r}\beta_{k}^{g-s}\gamma_{k}^{r}\overline{\gamma_{k}}^{s}\right]
.
Note that

\displaystyle\lvert\alpha_{k}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}\lvert c_{r}\rvert\frac{\lvert Z_{l}\rvert^{r}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{r}}\leq\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}\bigl\lvert\binom{p}{r}\bigr\rvert\frac{1}{(8p)^{r}}\kern 5.0pt.

The ratio of the
r+1
st term to the
r
th term in the above summation is,
\displaystyle\frac{\lvert p-r\rvert}{r+1}\cdot\frac{1}{8p}\leq 1/8
. Thus,
\lvert\alpha_{k}\rvert\leq\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}\bigl\lvert\binom{p}{r}\bigr\rvert\frac{1}{(8p)^{r}}\leq\left(\frac{p}{8p}\right)\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}(1/8)^{r-1}=(1/7)
. Hence,
\lvert\beta_{k}\rvert=\lvert\overline{\alpha_{k}}\rvert=\lvert\alpha_{k}\rvert\leq 1/7
.

Substituting in Eqn. (
A.5
) and taking absolute values, we obtain,

\displaystyle 0\leq

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\alpha_{k}+\gamma_{k}\right)^{e}\left(\beta_{k}+\overline{\gamma_{k}}\right)^{g}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha_{k}^{e}\beta_{k}^{g}\right]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r=0\ldots e,s=0\ldots g\\
r+s\geq 1\end{subarray}}d_{r}h_{s}\lvert\alpha_{k}\rvert^{e-r}\lvert\beta_{k}\rvert^{g-s}\zeta_{k}^{r+s}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{6p^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}\rho C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r=0\ldots e,s=0\ldots g\\
r+s\geq 1\end{subarray}}d_{r}h_{s}(1/7)^{e+g-r-s}\zeta_{k}^{r+s}\kern 5.0pt.

(97)

We now consider the summation term in Eqn. (
A.5
).
Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r=0\ldots e,s=0\ldots g\\
r+s\geq 1\end{subarray}}d_{r}h_{s}(1/7)^{e+g-r-s}\zeta_{k}^{r+s}

\displaystyle=(1/7)^{e+g}\sum_{s=1}^{g}h_{s}(7\zeta_{k})^{s}+(1/7)^{e+g}\sum_{r=1}^{e}d_{r}(7\zeta_{k})^{r}\sum_{s=0}^{g}h_{s}(7\zeta_{k})^{s}\kern 5.0pt.

(98)

Consider the summation
\sum_{s=1}^{g}h_{s}(7\zeta_{k})^{s}
. The ratio of the
s+1
th term to the
s
th term, for
s=1,2,\ldots,g-1
, is
\displaystyle\left(\frac{h_{s+1}}{h_{s}}\right)(7\zeta_{k})\leq 7g\zeta_{k}/2=n^{-\Omega(1)}
. Thus,

\sum_{s=1}^{g}h_{s}(7\zeta_{k})^{s}\leq h_{1}(7\zeta_{k})\sum_{s=1}^{g}(7g\zeta_{k}/2)^{s-1}=7g\zeta_{k}(1+O(g\zeta_{k}))\leq 8g\zeta_{k}\kern 5.0pt.

We now consider the second (double)-summation in Eqn. (
A.5
), namely,
\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{e}d_{r}(7\zeta_{k})^{r}\sum_{s=0}^{g}h_{s}(7\zeta_{k})^{s}
. Proceeding as in the previous paragraph, this is at most
8e\zeta_{k}
. The second summation,
\sum_{s=0}^{g}h_{s}(7\zeta_{k})^{s}=(1+7\zeta_{k})^{g}\leq\textrm{exp}\left\{{7\zeta_{k}g}\right\}\leq 1+8g\zeta_{k}
, since,
g=O(\log(1/\zeta))
and
\zeta_{k}=n^{-\Omega(1)}
. Therefore,
\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{e}d_{r}(7\zeta_{k})^{r}\sum_{s=0}^{g}h_{s}(7\zeta_{k})^{s}\leq 8e\zeta_{k}(1+8g\zeta_{k})
.

Substituting in Eqn. (
A.5
), we have,

\displaystyle(1/7)^{e+g}\sum_{s=1}^{g}h_{s}(7\zeta_{k})^{s}+(1/7)^{e+g}\sum_{r=1}^{e}d_{r}(7\zeta_{k})^{r}\sum_{s=0}^{g}h_{s}(7\zeta_{k})^{s}

\displaystyle\leq(1/7)^{e+g}(9)\zeta_{k}(e+g)

\displaystyle\leq(9)(e+g)(1/7)^{e+g}\left(\frac{F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}C}\right)^{k/2}\kern 5.0pt.

(99)

Substituting in Eqn. (
A.5
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\alpha_{k}+\gamma_{k}\right)^{e}\left(\beta_{k}+\overline{\gamma_{k}}\right)^{g}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{6p^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}\rho C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}+(9)(e+g)(1/7)^{e+g}\left(\frac{F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}C}\right)^{k/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{6p^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}\rho C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\cdot\left(1+9(e+g)\left(\frac{\rho}{6(7p)^{2}(e+g)}\right)^{(e+g)/2}\left(\frac{F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}C}\right)^{(k-(e+g))/2}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(100)

Let
d=2\lceil\log(1/\delta)\rceil
, so that
e+g\leq d
. Then,

\displaystyle 9(e+g)\left(\frac{\rho}{6(7p)^{2}(e+g)}\right)^{(e+g)/2}=\textrm{exp}\left\{{\ln(9(e+g))+\left(\frac{e+g}{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{\rho}{6(7p)^{2}(e+g)}\right)}\right\}\kern 5.0pt.

(101)

The function
x\ln\frac{\rho}{ax}
attains a maximum at
x=\frac{\rho}{ea}
and the maximum value is
\frac{\rho}{\textrm{exp}\left\{{1}\right\}a}
. Thus, the
RHS
in Eqn. (
101
) is bounded above by
\displaystyle\textrm{exp}\left\{{\ln(9d)+\frac{\rho}{\left(\textrm{exp}\left\{{1}\right\}\right)6(7p)^{2}}}\right\}
.
Further,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}C}\right)^{(k-(e+g))/2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{8p}\right)^{(k-(e+g))/2}

\displaystyle=\textrm{exp}\left\{{-(\ln(8p))(k-(e+g)/2)}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\textrm{exp}\left\{{-2-\left(\ln(9d)+\frac{\rho}{\left(\textrm{exp}\left\{{1}\right\}\right)6(7p)^{2}}\right)}\right\},

provided,
\displaystyle k\geq d+\left(\frac{1}{\ln(8p)}\right)\left(2+\left(\ln(9d)+\frac{\rho}{\left(\textrm{exp}\left\{{1}\right\}\right)6(7p)^{2}}\right)\right)=O(\log(1/\delta)+\log n)
, since,
\rho=O(\log n)
.

Under this condition,

\displaystyle 9(e+g)\left(\frac{\rho}{6(7p)^{2}(e+g)}\right)^{(e+g)/2}\left(\frac{F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}C}\right)^{(k-(e+g))/2}\leq e^{-2}\kern 5.0pt.

Substituting in Eqn. (
A.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\alpha_{k}+\gamma_{k}\right)^{e}\left(\beta_{k}+\overline{\gamma_{k}}\right)^{g}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{6p^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}\rho C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}\left(1+e^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{7p^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}\rho C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}

(102)

This proves the first statement of the lemma.

For the second statement of the lemma,
Y_{l}=\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}
.
Using
k
-wise independence of the
\omega_{lr}
’s family of random roots of unity, and since,
\lvert Z_{i}\rvert/\lvert x_{i}\rvert\leq\varrho=(B/C)^{1/2}\leq 1/(8p)
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{l}\right]

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}\left(\sum_{r=0}^{k-1}c_{r}\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{l}^{r}\right]}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{r}}+c_{k}\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{l}^{{}^{\prime}k}\right]}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{k}}\right)=\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}\left(1\pm\gamma\right)

since,
\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{l}^{r}\right]=0
, for
r\in[k-1]
and where,
\lvert\gamma\rvert\leq\lvert c_{k}\rvert(8p)^{-k}==\zeta
(say), which is
n^{-\Omega(1)}
since
k=\Omega(\log n)
. That is, we have shown that
\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{l}\right]-\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}\right\rvert\leq\gamma\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}
.

By a similar argument,
\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{l}}\right]=\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}(1\pm\gamma)
.

Since,
\displaystyle Y_{l}=(\lvert x_{l}\rvert+Z_{l})^{p}=\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{i}\rvert}\right)^{p}
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(Y_{l}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{l}\right]\right)^{e}\left(\overline{Y_{l}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{l}}\right]\right)^{g}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}(1+\gamma)\right)^{e}\left(\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}(1\pm\gamma)\right)^{g}\right]

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p(e+g)}(1+\zeta)^{e+g}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\mathbf{E}\left[\left((1+\gamma)^{-1}\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\left((1+\overline{\gamma})^{-1}\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-(1\pm\gamma)\right)^{g}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p(e+g)}(1+\gamma)^{e+g}\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\left(\left(1+\frac{\overline{Z_{l}}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{g}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p(e+g)}(1+\gamma)^{e+g}\left(\frac{7p^{2}(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2}\rho C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{2p-2}a(e+g)F_{2}^{\text{res}}\left({C}\right)}{\rho C}\right)^{(e+g)/2}

where,
a=7p^{2}(1+\gamma)^{e+g}\leq(7p^{2})e^{(e+g)\gamma}\leq 9p^{2}
, since,
e+g\leq O(\log(1/\delta)
and
\gamma=\textrm{exp}\left\{{-(\ln(8p))k}\right\}\leq O(1/(\log(1/\delta)))
, if
k=O(\log\log(1/\delta))
.
The second to last step follows from the first statement of the lemma.

Lemma 42

Let
1\leq e\leq\lceil\log(1/\delta)\rceil
and
l\in\text{mid}(G_{0})
. Suppose the random roots of unity family
\{\omega_{lr}\}_{l,r}
is
O(\log(n)\log(1/\delta))
-wise independent. Then,

\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq n^{-\Omega(1)}\kern 5.0pt.

Therefore,

\mathbf{E}\left[\left(Y_{l}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{l}\right]\right)^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{pe}n^{-\Omega(1)}

and

\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\overline{Y_{l}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{l}}\right]\right)^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{pe}n^{-\Omega(1)}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof:
All expectations in this proof are conditional on
\mathcal{H}
.
Let
k
be a parameter.
Let
c_{r}=\binom{p}{r}
, for
r=0,1,\ldots,k
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}c_{r}\left(\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{r}\right)+c_{k}\left(\frac{Z^{\prime}_{i}}{\lvert x_{i}\rvert}\right)^{k}\right)^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

(103)

where,
\lvert Z^{\prime}_{i}\rvert\leq\lvert Z_{i}\rvert
.

Denote
\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}c_{r}\left(\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{r}
by
\alpha
and
c_{k}\frac{Z_{i}^{{}^{\prime}k}}{\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{k}}
by
\beta
. Let
d_{r}=\binom{e}{r}
, for
r=0,1,\ldots,e
. We have,
\lvert\alpha\rvert\leq\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}\lvert c_{r}\rvert(8p)^{-1}\leq(1/8)\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}(1/8)^{r-1}=1/7
. Also,
\lvert\beta\rvert\leq(8p)^{-k}
.
Then, Eqn. (
103
) can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\alpha+\beta\right)^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]+\sum_{r=1}^{e}d_{r}\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha^{e-r}\beta^{r}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(104)

Now,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}c_{r}\left(\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{r}\right)^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\sum_{h_{1}+\ldots+h_{k-1}=e}\prod_{r=1}^{k-1}c_{r}^{h_{r}}\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{l}^{\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}r\cdot h_{r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}r\cdot h_{r}}}=0\kern 5.0pt.

Consider the sum
\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{e}d_{r}\lvert\alpha\rvert^{e-r}\lvert\beta\rvert^{r}
. The ratio of
r+1
th term to the
r
th term is
\displaystyle\left(\frac{e-r}{r+1}\right)\cdot 7\beta\leq(7e\lvert\beta\rvert/2)
. Assuming
k=O(\log n)
,
\lvert\beta\rvert\leq(8p)^{-k}=n^{-\Omega(1)}
. Therefore,
\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{e}d_{r}\lvert\alpha\rvert^{e-r}\lvert\beta\rvert^{r}\leq e(1/7)^{e-1}\lvert\beta\rvert(1+O(en^{-\Omega(1)}))\leq n^{-\Omega(1)}
.

Substituting these into Eqn. (
104
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\alpha+\beta\right)^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=0+\sum_{r=1}^{e}d_{r}\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha^{e-r}\beta^{r}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Taking absolute values, we have,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\right\rvert\leq\sum_{r=1}^{e}d_{r}\lvert\alpha\rvert^{e-r}\lvert\beta\rvert^{r}=n^{-\Omega(1)}\kern 5.0pt.

Now,
\displaystyle Y_{l}=\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}
and therefore,
\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{l}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}}-1\right\rvert\leq n^{-\Omega(1)}
, using
k=O(\log n)
-wise independence. Equivalently,
\displaystyle\bigl\lvert\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{l}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]-\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}\bigr\rvert\leq\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}n^{-\Omega(1)}
. So,
\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{l}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]=\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{p}(1+\gamma)
, where,
\lvert\gamma\rvert\leq n^{-\Omega(1)}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{l}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{l}\right])^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{pe}\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-(1+\gamma)\right)^{e}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Taking absolute values,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{l}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{l}\right])^{e}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{pe}\left\lvert\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-(1+\gamma)\right)^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{pe}(1+\lvert\gamma\rvert)^{e}\left\lvert\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{Z_{l}}{\lvert x_{l}\rvert}\right)^{p}-1\right)^{e}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{pe}(1+n^{-\Omega(1)})^{e}n^{-\Omega(1)}

\displaystyle\leq\lvert x_{l}\rvert^{pe}n^{-\Omega(1)}\kern 5.0pt.

since,
e\leq\lceil\log(1/\delta)\rceil\leq O(\log n)
.

A.6
A combinatorial lemma

In the calculation of the
d
th central moment for the contribution from the items in
\text{mid}(G_{0})
, we will need to estimate an upper bound on the following combinatorial sums defined in Eqns. (
3.3
) and (
2
) respectively.

\displaystyle Q(S_{1},S_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{\min(S_{1},S_{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S_{1}\\
e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}=S_{2}\\
g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1\end{subarray}}\binom{S_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}\binom{S_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}}

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q}\}}\prod_{r=1}^{q}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(p-1)(e_{r}+g_{r})}\prod_{r=1}^{q}(e_{r}+g_{r})^{(e_{r}+g_{r})/2}\kern 5.0pt.

\displaystyle R(S)

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor S/2\rfloor}\sum_{h_{1}+\ldots+h_{q}=S,h_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2}\binom{S}{h_{1},\ldots,h_{q}}\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q}\}}\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(p-1)h_{r}}\prod_{r\in[q]}h_{r}^{h_{r}/2}\kern 5.0pt.

Define the sum

\displaystyle P(S)

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{S}\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=2S\\
e_{1},\ldots e_{q}\geq 2\end{subarray}}\binom{S}{e_{1}/2,\ldots,e_{q}/2}\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(2p-2)(g_{r}/2)}\kern 5.0pt.

(105)

The expression in Eqn. (
3.3
) is upper bounded by

Lemma 43

[Re-statement of first part of Lemma
13
.]

Q(S_{1},S_{2})\leq R(S_{1}+S_{2})
.

Proof:

Consider the multinomial expansion of
(a_{1}+\ldots+a_{q})^{S_{1}}
, for
a_{1},\ldots,a_{q}\geq 0
together with the constraint that in each monomial of the form
\prod_{i\in[q]}{a_{i}}^{e_{i}}
, each
e_{i}\geq 1
. This equals
\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S_{1},e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1}\binom{S_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}\prod_{i\in[q]}a_{i}^{e_{i}}
. The multinomial expansion of
(a_{1}+\ldots+a_{q})^{S_{2}}
with the same constraint can be written as
\sum_{g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}=S_{2},g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1}

\binom{S_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}}\prod_{i\in[q]}a_{i}^{g_{i}}
. Now consider the multinomial expansion of
(a_{1}+\ldots+a_{q})^{S_{1}+S_{2}}
subject to
the constraint that in each monomial of the form
\prod_{i\in[q]}{a_{i}}^{h_{i}}
, each
h_{i}\geq 2
. This equals
\sum_{h_{1}+\ldots+h_{q}=S_{1}+S_{2},h_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2}

\binom{S_{1}+S_{2}}{h_{1},\ldots,h_{q}}\prod_{i\in[q]}a_{i}^{h_{i}}
. It therefore follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S_{1},e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1}\binom{S_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}\prod_{i\in[q]}a_{i}^{e_{i}}\sum_{g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}=S_{2},g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1}\binom{S_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}}\prod_{i\in[q]}a_{i}^{g_{i}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\leq\sum_{h_{1}+\ldots+h_{q}=S_{1}+S_{2},h_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2}\binom{S_{1}+S_{2}}{h_{1},\ldots,h_{q}}\prod_{i\in[q]}a_{i}^{h_{i}}

Here the variables
h_{i}
takes values (among other possibilities)
e_{i}+g_{i}
, for
i\in[q]
, for each
q
-partition vector
e
of
S_{1}
and
q
-partition
g
of
S_{2}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S_{1},e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1}\binom{S_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}\prod_{i\in[q]}a_{i}^{e_{i}}\sum_{g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}=S_{2},g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1}\binom{S_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}}\prod_{i\in[q]}a_{i}^{g_{i}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\prod_{i\in[q]}(e_{i}+g_{i})^{(e_{i}+g_{i})/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h_{1}+\ldots+h_{q}=S_{1}+S_{2},h_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2}\binom{S_{1}+S_{2}}{h_{1},\ldots,h_{q}}\prod_{i\in[q]}a_{i}^{h_{i}}\prod_{i\in[q]}h_{i}^{h_{i}/2}\kern 5.0pt.

(106)

It therefore follows that by letting
a_{i}=\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{p-1}
, for
i\in[n]
, that

\displaystyle Q(S_{1},S_{2})

(107)

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{\min(S_{1},S_{2})}\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q}\}\subset[n]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S_{1}\\
e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}=S_{2}\\
g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1\end{subarray}}\binom{S_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}\binom{S_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\prod_{r=1}^{q}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(p-1)(e_{r}+g_{r})}\prod_{r=1}^{q}(e_{r}+g_{r})^{(e_{r}+g_{r})/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor(S_{1}+S_{2})/2\rfloor}\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q}\}}\sum_{h_{1}+\ldots+h_{q}=S_{1}+S_{2},h_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2}\binom{S_{1}+S_{2}}{h_{1},\ldots,h_{q}}\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(p-1)h_{r}}\prod_{r\in[q]}h_{r}^{h_{r}/2}

\displaystyle=R(S_{1}+S_{2})

(108)

Suppose we generalize the factorial notation
x!
to mean
\Gamma(x+1)
, when
x
is a fraction of the form
(n+1/2)
, for
n\geq 1
. Using this, generalize the multinomial coefficient notation
\binom{S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}
to denote
\frac{S!}{e_{1}!\ldots e_{q}!}
, even when the
e_{j}
’s are fractional and of the form
(n+1/2)
, for
n\geq 1
.

Lemma 44

R^{2}(S)\leq R(2S)
.

Proof:

\displaystyle R^{2}(S)

\displaystyle=\sum_{q_{1}=1}^{\lfloor S/2\rfloor}\sum_{q_{2}=1}^{\lfloor S/2\rfloor}\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q_{1}}\}}\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{q_{2}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q_{1}}=S\\
e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q_{2}}=S\\
g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2\end{subarray}}\binom{S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q_{1}}}\binom{S}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{q_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\prod_{r\in[q_{1}]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(p-1)e_{r}}\prod_{s\in[q_{2}]}\lvert x_{j_{s}}\rvert^{(p-1)g_{s}}\prod_{r\in[q_{1}]}e_{r}^{e_{r}/2}\prod_{r\in[q_{2}]}g_{r}^{g_{r}/2}\kern 5.0pt.

Define

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=\bigl\lvert\{i_{1},\ldots i_{q_{1}}\}\cap\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{q_{2}}\}\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=\{\bigl\lvert\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q_{1}}\}\setminus\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{q_{2}}\}\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle=\{\bigl\lvert\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{q_{2}}\}\setminus\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q_{1}}\}\bigr\rvert

Then,
R^{2}(S)
can be written as

\displaystyle R^{2}(S)

\displaystyle=\sum_{a=0}^{S}\sum_{b=\max(0,1-a)}^{S-a}\sum_{c=\max(0,1-a)}^{S-a}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{a+b}=S\\
e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{a+c}=S\\
g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2\end{subarray}}\binom{S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{a+b}}\binom{S}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{a+c}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{a},j_{1},\ldots,j_{b},k_{1},\ldots,k_{c}\}}\prod_{l=1}^{a}\lvert x_{i_{l}}\rvert^{(p-1)(e_{l}+g_{l})}\prod_{m=1}^{b}\lvert x_{j_{m}}\rvert^{(p-1)e_{l+m}}\prod_{n=1}^{c}\lvert x_{k_{n}}\rvert^{(p-1)e_{l+n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 144.54pt\prod_{l=1}^{a}\left(e_{l}^{e_{l}/2}g_{l}^{g_{l}/2}\right)\prod_{m=1}^{b}e_{l+m}^{e_{l+m}/2}\prod_{n=1}^{x}g_{l+n}^{g_{l+n}/2}\kern 5.0pt.

(109)

By a similar argument as in Lemma
43
, and using the fact that
\left(e_{l}^{e_{l}/2}g_{l}^{g_{l}/2}\right)\leq(e_{l}+g_{l})^{(e_{l}+g_{l})/2}
, for any
e_{l},g_{l}\geq 1
, we have that the sum in Eqn. (
109
), is bounded above by the following sum (using
q=a+b+c
).

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q=1}^{S}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=2S\\
e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2\end{subarray}}\binom{2S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q}\}}\prod_{l=1}^{q}\lvert x_{i_{l}}\rvert^{(p-1)e_{j}}\prod_{l=1}^{q}e_{l}^{e_{l}/2}

(110)

\displaystyle=R(2S)\kern 5.0pt.

Lemma 45

\displaystyle R(2S)

\displaystyle\leq(2eS)^{S}P(S)

\displaystyle=(2eS)^{S}\sum_{q=1}^{S}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=2S\\
e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2\end{subarray}}\binom{S}{e_{1}/2,\ldots,e_{q}/2}\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots i_{q}\}}\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(2p-2)(e_{r}/2)}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof:

Consider Eqn. (
110
).

Fix
q
and
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q}\}
. Then, using Stirling’s formula, we have,

\displaystyle\binom{2S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}\prod_{l=1}^{q}e_{l}^{e_{l}/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{(2S)!}{e_{1}!\ldots e_{q}!}\prod_{l=1}^{q}e_{l}^{e_{l}/2}

\displaystyle\leq(2S)^{S}\left(\frac{S!e^{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}}}{\left(\prod_{r\in[q]}\sqrt{2\pi e_{r}}\right)e_{1}^{e_{1}}\cdots e_{q}^{e_{q}}}\right)\prod_{l=1}^{q}e_{l}^{e_{l}/2}

\displaystyle\leq(2eS)^{S}\left(\frac{S!}{(e_{1}/2)!\ldots(e_{q}/2)!}\right)

\displaystyle=(2eS)^{S}\binom{S}{e_{1}/2,\ldots,e_{q}/2}

using the generalized factorial notation.
The statement of the lemma now follows.

We would now like to establish a bound on
R(2S)
in terms of
F_{2p-2}^{S}
. The two expressions are shown below.

\displaystyle P(S)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q=1}^{S}\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots i_{q}\}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}=2S\\
g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2\end{subarray}}\binom{S}{g_{1}/2,\ldots,g_{q}/2}\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(2p-2)(g_{r}/2)}\kern 5.0pt.

(111)

\displaystyle F_{2p-2}^{S}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{S}\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots i_{q}\}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S\\
e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1\end{subarray}}\binom{S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(2p-2)e_{r}}\kern 5.0pt.

(112)

Lemma 46

P(S)\leq 4^{S}F_{2p-2}^{S}
.

Corollary 47

R(2S)\leq(8eS)^{S}F_{2p-2}^{S}
.

Proof:
[Proof of Corollary
47
.]
We have from Lemma
45
that
R(2S)\leq(2eS)^{S}P(S)
. By Lemma
46
, we have
P(S)\leq 4^{S}F_{2p-2}^{S}
. Combining, we obtain the corollary.

Proof:
[Of Lemma
46
.]

Fix
q
and fix a choice of the index set
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q}\}\subset[n]
.

For
q\in[S]
, define the sets
U_{q}=\{(g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}):g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}=2S,g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2\}
and
V_{q}=\{(e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}):e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S,e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1\}
. The sets
U_{q}
and
V_{q}
may be viewed as two partitions of bi-partite graph
G_{q}=(U_{q},V_{q},E_{q})
, where the edge-set
E_{q}
is defined as follows.
Let
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{q})\in U_{q}
. Let
h
be the number of indices
j
such that
g_{j}
is even and let these indices be
k_{1}<\ldots<k_{h}
in sequence. Thus,
g_{j}
is odd for any
j\not\in\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{h}\}
.
Define,

\displaystyle e_{k_{j}}=g_{k_{j}}/2,~~~j=1,2,\ldots,h\kern 5.0pt.

(113)

Note that
q-h
is even. This is because
0=2S\mod 2=g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}\mod 2=\left((g_{k_{1}}+\ldots+g_{k_{h}}\right)\mod 2+\sum_{j\not\in\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{h}\}}(g_{j}\mod 2)=q-h\mod 2
, since each of
g_{k_{r}}
is even, for
r\in[h]
and each of
g_{j}
is odd, for
j\in[q]\setminus\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{h}\}
.

Let
l_{1}<l_{2}<\ldots<l_{q-h}
be the sequence of all indices in
[q]
such that
g_{l_{j}}
is odd, for
l\in[q-h]
. Note that
q-h
is even. Let
t
be the permutation of
\{l_{1},\ldots,l_{q-h}\}
such that

\lvert x_{t_{1}}\rvert\leq\lvert x_{t_{2}}\rvert\leq\ldots\leq\lvert x_{t_{q-h}}\rvert
. Define,

\displaystyle e_{t_{j}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\lfloor g_{t_{j}}/2\rfloor&~~~j=1,2,\ldots,(q-h)/2\\
\lceil g_{t_{j}}/2\rceil&~~~j=(q-h)/2+1,\ldots,q-h\kern 5.0pt.\end{cases}

(114)

Denote this mapping as
\phi(g_{1},\ldots,g_{q})
, where,
\phi:U_{q}\rightarrow V_{q}
.

We would now like to count
\lvert\phi^{-1}(e_{1},\ldots,e_{q})\rvert
. Fix
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{q})
and fix any
H\subset[q]
. We will let
H
be the set of indices such that
g_{i}
is even and
g_{i}=2e_{i}
. Let
\lvert H\rvert=h
. Let
l_{1}<l_{2}<\ldots<l_{q-h}
be the sequence of indices in
[q]\setminus H
. Let
t
be the permutation of
\{l_{1},\ldots,l_{q-h}\}
such that

\lvert x_{t_{1}}\rvert\leq\lvert x_{t_{2}}\rvert\leq\ldots\leq\lvert x_{t_{q-h}}\rvert
. Define,

\displaystyle g_{t_{j}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}2e_{t_{j}}-1&\text{ for }j=(q-h)/2+1,\ldots,q-h\\
2e_{t_{j}}+1&\text{ for }j=1,2,\ldots,(q-h)/2\kern 5.0pt.\end{cases}

(115)

For a fixed
H
, this function
\psi_{H}(e_{1},\ldots,e_{q})
is a mapping from
V_{q}
to
U_{q}
. Further,
if for a given
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{q})
,
H=\{i:g_{i}\text{ is even }\}
, then,

\psi_{H}(\phi(g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}))=(g_{1},\ldots,g_{q})\kern 5.0pt.

Hence,

\displaystyle\lvert\phi^{-1}(e_{1},\ldots,e_{q})\rvert\leq\bigl\lvert\{H:H\subset[q]\}\bigr\rvert=2^{q}\kern 5.0pt.

(116)

Another important fact is that by construction, for any
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{q})\in U_{q}
,
if we let
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{q})=\phi(g_{1},\ldots,g_{q})
, then,

\prod_{r=1}^{q}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(p-1)g_{r}}\leq\prod_{r=1}^{q}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(2p-2)e_{r}}\kern 5.0pt.

This can be seen as follows. Let
H=\{i:g_{i}\text{ is even }\}
and let
h=\lvert H\rvert
. Let
\bar{H}=[q]\setminus H
and
\bigl\lvert\bar{H}\bigr\rvert=q-h
. Then,

\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{r}\rvert^{(p-1)g_{r}}=\prod_{r\in H}\lvert x_{r}\rvert^{(2p-2)e_{r}}\prod_{r\in\bar{H}}\lvert x_{r}\rvert^{(p-1)g_{r})}
. It suffices to show that
\prod_{r\in\bar{H}}\lvert x_{r}\rvert^{g_{r}}\leq\prod_{r\in\bar{H}}\lvert x_{r}\rvert^{2e_{r}}
. This is equivalent to show that
\sum_{r\in\bar{H}}g_{r}\ln\lvert x_{r}\rvert\leq\sum_{r\in\bar{H}}2e_{r}\ln\lvert x_{r}\rvert
. Let
\alpha_{r}=\ln\lvert x_{r}\rvert\geq 0
. Let
t
be a permutation of the indices of the elements of
\bar{H}
such that
\lvert x_{t_{1}}\rvert\leq\lvert x_{t_{2}}\rvert\leq\ldots\leq\lvert x_{t_{q-h}}\rvert
. So it suffices to show that
\sum_{r=1}^{q-h}g_{t_{r}}\alpha_{t_{r}}\leq\sum_{r=1}^{q-h}2e_{t_{r}}\alpha_{t_{r}}
. The permutation orders the indices so that
\alpha_{t_{1}}\leq\alpha_{t_{2}}\leq\ldots\leq\alpha_{t_{q-h}}
. The sum
L(x)=\sum_{r=1}^{q-h}x_{r}\alpha_{t_{r}}
is a linear function of
g_{t_{r}}
. Consider the assignment for the
x_{r}
’s, where,
x_{r}=g_{t_{r}}
, where,
g_{t_{r}}\geq 2
is odd for each
r
. For any
j\in 1,\ldots,(q-h)/2
, let
x^{\prime}_{r}=g_{t_{r}}-1
and
x^{\prime}_{q-h-r}=g_{t_{q-h-r}}+1
and let
x^{\prime}_{j}
equal
x_{j}
for all other indices
j\in[q-h]
. Then,
L(x)\leq L(x^{\prime})
, since,
\alpha_{t_{j}}
’s are in non-descending order. Continuing this argument for each
r\in[(q-h)/2]
, we obtain that
L(x)\leq L(x^{*})
, where,
x^{*}_{r}=g_{t_{r}}-1=2e_{t_{r}}
, for
r=1,2,\ldots,(q-h)/2
and
x^{*}_{r}=g_{t_{r}}+1=2e_{t_{r}}
, for
r=1,2,\ldots,(q-h)/2
. This proves the assertion.

Let
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{q})=\phi(g_{1},\ldots,g_{q})
, for some
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{q})\in U_{q}
.
We are now interested in an upper bound for the following ratio

\displaystyle\cfrac{\binom{S}{g_{1}/2,\ldots,g_{q}/2}}{\binom{S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}}
.
As before, let
H=\{i\in[q]:g_{i}\text{ is even }\}
and let
E=\sum_{i\in H}(g_{i}/2)
. Let
the indices in
H
be
k_{1}<k_{2}<\ldots<k_{h}
, where,
h=\lvert H\rvert
. Let the remaining indices be
l_{1}<l_{2}<\ldots,l_{h^{\prime}}
, where,
h^{\prime}=(q-h)
. Then,

\displaystyle\binom{S}{g_{1}/2,\ldots,g_{q}/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{S!}{(g_{1}/2)!\cdots(g_{q}/2)!}

\displaystyle=\binom{S}{E}\binom{E}{(g_{k_{1}}/2),\ldots,(g_{k_{h}}/2)}\binom{S-E}{(g_{l_{1}}/2)\ldots,(g_{l_{h^{\prime}}}/2)}

\displaystyle=\binom{S}{E}\binom{E}{e_{k_{1}},\ldots,e_{k_{h}}}\binom{S-E}{(g_{l_{1}}/2)\ldots,(g_{l_{h^{\prime}}}/2)}

since,

(e_{1},\ldots,e_{q})=\phi(g_{1},\ldots,g_{q})
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\binom{S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}

\displaystyle=\binom{S}{E}\binom{E}{e_{k_{1}},\ldots,e_{k_{h}}}\binom{S-E}{e_{l_{1}}\ldots,e_{l_{h^{\prime}}}}

Taking ratios, let
R
denote the ratio
\displaystyle\cfrac{\binom{S}{g_{1}/2,\ldots,g_{q}/2}}{\binom{S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}}
.

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle=\cfrac{\binom{S}{g_{1}/2,\ldots,g_{q}/2}}{\binom{S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}}=\cfrac{\binom{S-E}{(g_{l_{1}}/2)\ldots,(g_{l_{h^{\prime}}}/2)}}{\binom{S-E}{e_{l_{1}}\ldots,e_{l_{h^{\prime}}}}}=\prod_{r=1}^{h^{\prime}/2}\left(\cfrac{e_{l_{r}}!}{g_{l_{r}}/2!}\right)\left(\cfrac{e_{l_{h^{\prime}-r+1}}!}{g_{l_{h^{\prime}-r+1}}/2!}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{r=1}^{h^{\prime}/2}\cfrac{e_{l_{r}}!}{(e_{l_{r}}+1/2)!}\prod_{r=h^{\prime}/2+1}^{h^{\prime}}\cfrac{e_{l_{r}}!}{(e_{l_{r}}-1/2)!}\leq\prod_{r=h^{\prime}/2+1}^{h^{\prime}}\cfrac{e_{l_{r}}!}{(e_{l_{r}}-1/2)!}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{r=h^{\prime}/2+1}^{h^{\prime}}\cfrac{e_{l_{r}}!}{(e_{l_{r}}-1)!}\leq\prod_{r=h^{\prime}/2+1}^{h^{\prime}}e_{l_{r}}\leq\prod_{r=1}^{h^{\prime}}e_{l_{r}}\kern 5.0pt.

(117)

Further,
\sum_{r=1}^{h^{\prime}}e_{l_{r}}=S-E

=S^{\prime}
(say). Hence,
\prod_{l=1}^{h^{\prime}}e_{l_{r}}
is maximized when the
e_{l_{r}}
’s equals
S^{\prime}/h
. The product is then at most
(S^{\prime}/h^{\prime})^{h^{\prime}}=\textrm{exp}\left\{{h^{\prime}\ln(S^{\prime}/h^{\prime})}\right\}
.
The function
g(x)=x\ln\frac{a}{x}
attains its maximum in the range
1\leq x\leq a
at
x=a/e
. The corresponding maximum value is
g^{*}=a/e
.
Therefore,
\displaystyle\textrm{exp}\left\{{h^{\prime}\ln(S^{\prime}/h^{\prime})}\right\}\leq\textrm{exp}\left\{{S/e}\right\}
.
Substituting in Eqn. (
A.6
), we have,

\displaystyle R=\cfrac{\binom{S}{g_{1}/2,\ldots,g_{q}/2}}{\binom{S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}}\leq e^{(S-E)/e}\kern 5.0pt.

(118)

Fix
q\in[S]
and index set
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q}\}\subset[n]
. We will now try to relate the sub-summations from Eqn. (
111
) and Eqn. (
112
) respectively.

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}=2S\\
g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2\end{subarray}}\binom{S}{g_{1}/2,\ldots,g_{q}/2}\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(2p-2)(g_{r}/2)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S\\
e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}=2S\\
g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2\\
\phi(g_{1},\ldots,g_{q})=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{q})\end{subarray}}\binom{S}{g_{1}/2,\ldots,g_{q}/2}\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(2p-2)(g_{r}/2)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S\\
e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}=2S\\
g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2\\
\phi(g_{1},\ldots,g_{q})=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{q})\end{subarray}}e^{S/e}\binom{S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}/2}\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(2p-2)e_{r}}

\displaystyle=e^{S/e}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S\\
e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1\end{subarray}}\binom{S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}/2}\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(2p-2)e_{r}}\left\lvert\phi^{-1}(e_{1},\ldots,e_{q})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq(2e^{1/e})^{S}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S\\
e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1\end{subarray}}\binom{S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}/2}\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(2p-2)e_{r}}\kern 5.0pt.

(119)

Therefore, since,
q\leq S
,

\displaystyle P(S)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q=1}^{S}\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots i_{q}\}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}=2S\\
g_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 2\end{subarray}}\binom{S}{g_{1}/2,\ldots,g_{q}/2}\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(2p-2)(g_{r}/2)}

\displaystyle\leq(2e^{1/e})^{S}\sum_{q=1}^{S}\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots i_{q}\}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S\\
e_{j}^{\prime}s\geq 1\end{subarray}}\binom{S}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}/2}\prod_{r\in[q]}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(2p-2)e_{r}}

\displaystyle\leq 4^{S}F_{2p-2}^{S}\kern 5.0pt.

Corollary 48

[Re-statement of second part of Lemma
13
.]

R(S)\leq\left(4e^{1+1/e}SF_{2p-2}\right)^{S/2}
.

Proof:

Follows from Lemma
47
and Lemma
44
.

th central moment calculations for

Lemma 49

Let
a\geq 9p^{2}
be a constant and let
C\geq(400)ae(n^{1-2/p}/\log(n))\epsilon^{-2}\log(1/\delta)
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{\min(S_{1},S_{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S_{1}\\
e_{j}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}=S_{2}\\
g_{j}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\binom{S_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}\binom{S_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots i_{q}\}}\prod_{r=1}^{q}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{p(e_{r}+g_{r})-2((e_{r}+g_{r})/2)}\left(\frac{aF_{2}}{\rho C}\right)^{\sum_{r=1}^{q}(e_{r}+g_{r})/2}\prod_{r=1}^{q}(e_{r}+g_{r})^{(e_{r}+g_{r})/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{20}\right)^{S_{1}+S_{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof:

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{\min(S_{1},S_{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S_{1}\\
e_{j}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q}=S_{2}\\
g_{j}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\binom{S_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}\binom{S_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots i_{q}\}}\prod_{r=1}^{q}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{p(e_{r}+g_{r})-2((e_{r}+g_{r})/2)}\left(\frac{aF_{2}}{\rho C}\right)^{\sum_{r=1}^{q}(e_{r}+g_{r})/2}\prod_{r=1}^{q}(e_{r}+g_{r})^{(e_{r}+g_{r})/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{aF_{2}}{\rho C}\right)^{(S_{1}+S_{2})/2}Q(S_{1}+S_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{aF_{2}}{\rho C}\right)^{(S_{1}+S_{2})/2}R(S_{1}+S_{2}),~~\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lem:comb:1}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{aF_{2}}{\rho C}\right)^{(S_{1}+S_{2})/2}\left(16e(S_{1}+S_{2})F_{2p-2}\right)^{(S_{1}+S_{2})/2},~~\text{ by Corollary~\ref{lem:comb:R2SF2p2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{16ae(S_{1}+S_{2})F_{2}}{\rho C}\right)^{(S_{1}+S_{2})/2}F_{p}^{(2-2/p)(S_{1}+S_{2})/2}

(120)

For
p\geq 2
,
2p-2\geq p
and so
F_{2p-2}\leq F_{p}^{2-2/p}
. Since,
\rho\geq\log n
,
1\leq S_{1},S_{2}\leq\lceil\log(1/\delta)\rceil
and

C=K(n^{1-2/p}/\log(n))\epsilon^{-2}\log(1/\delta))
, we have, for
K=(400)(16ae)
, that

\displaystyle\frac{16ae(S_{1}+S_{2})aF_{2}}{(\log n)K(n^{1-2/p}/\log(n))\epsilon^{-2}\log(1/\delta)}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}F_{p}^{2/p}}{400}\kern 5.0pt.

Substituting in Eqn. (
A.7
), we have,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{16ae(S_{1}+S_{2})F_{2}}{\rho C}\right)^{(S_{1}+S_{2})/2}F_{p}^{(2-2/p)(S_{1}+S_{2})/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{20}\right)^{S_{1}+S_{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

Let
\mathcal{H}
denote the event
\mathcal{G}\wedge\textsc{nocollision}\wedge\textsc{goodest}
.

Lemma 50
(Re-statement of Lemma
14
)

.
Let
a\geq 9p^{2}
be a constant and let

\epsilon^{-2}\log(1/\delta)

\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i\in\text{mid}(G_{0})}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in\text{mid}(G_{0})}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{2}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{10}\right)^{d_{1}+d_{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

\mathcal{H}

Let

.

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i\in\text{mid}(G_{0})}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{G}\right])\right)^{d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in\text{mid}(G_{0})}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=0}^{\min(d_{1},d_{2})}\sum_{s=\alpha_{0}(q)}^{d_{1}-q}\sum_{t=\alpha_{0}(q)}^{d_{2}-q}\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q+s}=d_{1}}\binom{d_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q+s}}\sum_{g_{1}+\ldots+g_{q+t}=d_{2}}\binom{d_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{q+s}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q},j_{1},\ldots,j_{s},k_{1},\ldots,k_{t}\}}\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{r=1}^{q}(Y_{i_{r}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{r}}(\overline{Y_{i_{r}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{r}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{r}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt\left.\prod_{l=1}^{s}(Y_{j_{l}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{l}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{q+s}}\prod_{m=1}^{t}(Y_{k_{m}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{k_{m}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{q+t}}\right]

(121)

Note that for a given value of

, if

, then

and

are at least 1, and otherwise, for

,

and

may be initialized from 0.

Consider the term in the expectation of Eqn. (
A.7
). By

and its consequent property as explained earlier,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{r=1}^{q}(Y_{i_{r}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{r}}(\overline{Y_{i_{r}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{r}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{r}}\prod_{l=1}^{s}(Y_{j_{l}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{l}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{q+s}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 72.26999pt\prod_{m=1}^{t}(Y_{k_{m}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{k_{m}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{q+t}}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{r=1}^{q}\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{i_{r}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{r}}(\overline{Y_{i_{r}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{r}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{r}}\prod_{l=1}^{s}\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{j_{l}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{l}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{q+s}}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 54.2025pt\left.\prod_{m=1}^{t}\mathbf{E}\left[(Y_{k_{m}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{k_{m}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{q+t}}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{r=1}^{q}\left(\frac{\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{2p-2}a(e_{r}+g_{r})F_{2}}{\rho C}\right)^{(e_{r}+g_{r})/2}\prod_{l=1}^{s}\lvert x_{j_{l}}\rvert^{pe_{q+l}}n^{-\Omega(e_{q+l})}\prod_{m=1}^{t}\lvert x_{k_{m}}\rvert^{pg_{q+m}}n^{-\Omega(g_{q+m})}

(122)

by Lemmas
12
and
42
.

Let
S_{1}=e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}

Rewrite Eqn. (

\displaystyle=\sum_{S_{1}=0}^{d_{1}}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S_{2}=0\\

\displaystyle\hskip 144.54pt\binom{d_{2}-S_{2}}{g_{q+1},\ldots,g_{q+s}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q},j_{1},\ldots,j_{s},k_{1},\ldots,k_{t}\}}\mathbf{E}\left[\prod_{r=1}^{q}(Y_{i_{r}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i_{r}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{r}}(\overline{Y_{i_{r}}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i_{r}}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{r}}\prod_{l=1}^{s}(Y_{j_{l}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{j_{l}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{e_{q+s}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 144.54pt\left.\prod_{m=1}^{t}(Y_{k_{m}}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{k_{m}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])^{g_{q+t}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S_{1}=0}^{d_{1}}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S_{2}=0\\
S_{1}=0\Rightarrow S_{2}=0\end{subarray}}^{d_{2}}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q=0\\

\displaystyle\hskip 57.81621pt\binom{d_{1}}{S_{1}}\binom{S_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}\binom{d_{1}-S_{1}}{e_{q+1},\ldots,e_{q+s}}\binom{d_{2}}{S_{2}}\binom{S_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}}\binom{d_{2}-S_{2}}{g_{q+1},\ldots,g_{q+s}}

(124)

\displaystyle P_{1}(S_{1},S_{2})\leq\left(\epsilon F_{p}/20\right)^{S_{1}+S_{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

We note that the summation in Eqn. (

\displaystyle\sum_{S_{1}=0}^{d_{1}}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S_{2}=0\\
S_{1}=0\Rightarrow S_{2}=0\end{subarray}}^{d_{2}}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q=0\\
S_{1}=0\Rightarrow q=0\end{subarray}}^{\min(d_{1},d_{2})}~~\sum_{s=\alpha_{0}(q)}^{d_{1}-S_{1}}\sum_{t=\alpha_{0}(q)}^{d_{2}-S_{2}}\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S_{1}}\sum_{e_{q+1},\ldots,e_{q+s}=d_{1}-S_{1}}

\displaystyle\binom{d_{1}}{S_{1}}\binom{S_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}\binom{d_{1}-S_{1}}{e_{q+1},\ldots,e_{q+s}}\binom{d_{2}}{S_{2}}\binom{S_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}}\binom{d_{2}-S_{2}}{g_{q+1},\ldots,g_{q+s}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q},j_{1},\ldots,j_{s},k_{1},\ldots,k_{t}\}}\prod_{r=1}^{q}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(p-1)(e_{r}+q_{r})}\prod_{l=1}^{s}\lvert x_{j_{l}}\rvert^{pe_{q+l}}n^{-\Omega(e_{q+l})}\prod_{m=1}^{t}\lvert x_{k_{m}}\rvert^{pg_{q+m}}n^{-\Omega(g_{q+m})}

(126)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S_{1}=0}^{d_{1}}\binom{d_{1}}{S_{1}}\sum_{S_{2}=0}^{d_{2}}\binom{d_{2}}{S_{2}}~~\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q=0\\
S_{1}=0\Rightarrow q=0\end{subarray}}^{\min(d_{1},d_{2})}~~\sum_{s=\alpha_{0}(q)}^{d_{1}-S_{1}}\sum_{t=\alpha_{0}(q)}^{d_{2}-S_{2}}\sum_{e_{1}+\ldots+e_{q}=S_{1}}\sum_{e_{q+1},\ldots,e_{q+s}=d_{1}-S_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\binom{S_{1}}{e_{1},\ldots,e_{q}}\binom{d_{1}-S_{1}}{e_{q+1},\ldots,e_{q+s}}\binom{S_{2}}{g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}}\binom{d_{2}-S_{2}}{g_{q+1},\ldots,g_{q+s}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q}\}}\sum_{\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{s}\}}\sum_{\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{t}\}}\prod_{r=1}^{q}\lvert x_{i_{r}}\rvert^{(p-1)(e_{r}+q_{r})}\prod_{l=1}^{s}\lvert x_{j_{l}}\rvert^{pe_{q+l}}n^{-\Omega(e_{q+l})}\prod_{m=1}^{t}\lvert x_{k_{m}}\rvert^{pg_{q+m}}n^{-\Omega(g_{q+m})}

(127)

and

and

) is bounded above as follows.

\displaystyle\sum_{S_{1}=0}^{d_{1}}\binom{d_{1}}{S_{1}}\sum_{S_{2}=0}^{d_{2}}\binom{d_{2}}{S_{2}}P_{1}(S_{1},S_{2})P_{2}(d_{1},S_{1})P_{2}(d_{2},S_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S_{1}=0}^{d_{1}}\binom{d_{1}}{S_{1}}\sum_{S_{2}=0}^{d_{2}}\binom{d_{2}}{S_{2}}\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{20}\right)^{S_{1}+S_{2}}\left(n^{-\Omega(1)}F_{p}\right)^{d_{1}+d_{2}-S_{1}-S_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{10}\right)^{d_{1}+d_{2}}

Putting things together

The following lemma, which is a restatement of Lemma
15
is applied for the case when
\delta=O(n^{-c})
, for some constant
c
, but holds generally. Recall, that as stated, for the case
\delta=n^{-\Theta(1)}
, the shelf structure is not needed.
For the statement and proof of Lemma
15
, let
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{G}\wedge\textsc{nocollision}\wedge\textsc{goodest}
.

Lemma 51
(Re-statement of Lemma
15
)

Let
C\geq Kn^{1-2/p}\epsilon^{-2}\log(1/\delta)/\log(n)+Ln^{1-2/p}\epsilon^{-4/p}\\
\log^{2/p}(1/\delta)
, where,
K
is the constant from Lemma
14
and
L
is the constant from Lemma
11
. Let
B
be such that
C/B\geq(5p)^{2}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d}\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{5}\right)^{2d}

Proof:
Note that in this proof, the terms such as
\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\right]
or
\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\right]
are written for brevity, they should be interpreted as
\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]
and
\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]
respectively.

Let
S_{1}=\text{lmargin}(G_{0})\cup_{l=1}^{L}G_{l}
. Recall that for the case
\delta=n^{-O(1)}
,
\text{mid}(G_{0})=\{i:\lvert x_{i}\rvert\geq T_{0}(1+\bar{\epsilon})\}
, corresponding to the frequency range
[T_{0}(1+\bar{\epsilon}),\infty)
.

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i\in S}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\sum_{i\in\text{mid}(G_{0})}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)+\left(\sum_{i\in S_{1}}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)\right)^{d}

\displaystyle=\sum_{d_{1}=0}^{d}\binom{d}{d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in\text{mid}(G_{0})}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in S_{1}}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d-d_{1}}

Similarly,

\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d}=\sum_{d_{2}=0}^{d}\binom{d}{d_{2}}\left(\sum_{i\in\text{mid}(G_{0})}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{2}}\\
\left(\sum_{i\in S_{1}}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d-d_{2}}

Taking the product and then its expectation, we obtain,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d}\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{d_{1}=0}^{d}\sum_{d_{2}=0}^{d}\binom{d}{d_{1}}\binom{d}{d_{2}}\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i\in\text{mid}(G_{0})}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in\text{mid}(G_{0})}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\left.\left(\sum_{i\in S_{1}}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d-d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in S_{1}}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d-d_{2}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(128)

We now consider the expectation term in Eqn. (
128
). By properties of
nocollision
as discussed earlier, we have,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i\in\text{mid}(G_{0})}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in\text{mid}(G_{0})}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 36.135pt\left(\sum_{i\in S_{1}}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d-d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in S_{1}}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d-d_{2}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i\in\text{mid}(G_{0})}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in\text{mid}(G_{0})}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d_{2}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i\in S_{1}}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d-d_{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in S_{1}}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d-d_{2}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{10}\right)^{d_{1}+d_{2}}\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{10}\right)^{d-d_{1}+d-d_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{10}\right)^{2d}

(129)

where, the second to last equation follows from Lemmas
14
and
11
respectively.
Substituting in Eqn. (
128
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d}\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\mid\mathcal{H}\right])\right)^{d}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{10}\right)^{2d}\sum_{d_{1}=0}^{d}\sum_{d_{2}=0}^{d}\binom{d}{d_{1}}\binom{d}{d_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{10}\right)^{2d}2^{2d}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{5}\right)^{2d}\kern 5.0pt.

Lemma 52
( Second part of Lemma
15
restated.)

Let
C\geq Kn^{1-2/p}\epsilon^{-2}\log(1/\delta)/\log(n)+Ln^{1-2/p}\allowbreak\epsilon^{-4/p}\log^{2/p}(1/\delta)
, where,
K,L
are suitable constants.
Then, for
d=\lceil\log(1/\delta)\rceil
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\biggl[\biggl(\sum_{i\in[n]}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\right])\biggr)^{d}\biggl(\sum_{i\in[n]}(\overline{Y_{i}}-\mathbf{E}\left[\overline{Y_{i}}\right])\biggr)^{d}\mid\mathcal{H}\biggr]\leq\biggl(\frac{\epsilon F_{p}}{5}\biggr)^{2d}

It follows that

\text{Pr}\left[\left\lvert\sum_{i\in[n]}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\right])\right\rvert\geq(\epsilon/2)F_{p}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]\leq\delta^{2}
. Hence, for
\delta=n^{-O(1)}
,

\text{Pr}\left[\left\lvert\sum_{i\in[n]}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\right])\right\rvert\geq(\epsilon/2)F_{p}\right]\leq\delta

Proof:

\displaystyle\text{Pr}\left[\left\lvert\sum_{i\in[n]}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\right])\right\rvert\geq(\epsilon/2)F_{p}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\text{Pr}\left[\left\lvert\sum_{i\in[n]}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\right])\right\rvert^{2d}\geq(\epsilon F_{p}/2)^{2d}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbf{E}\left[\left\lvert\sum_{i\in[n]}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\right])\right\rvert^{2d}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]}{(\epsilon F_{p}/2)^{2d}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(\epsilon F_{p}/2)^{2d}}\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[Y_{i}\right])\right)^{d}\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}(\bar{Y}_{i}-\mathbf{E}\left[\bar{Y}_{i}\right])\right)^{d}\mid\mathcal{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(\epsilon F_{p}/5\right)^{2d}}{(\epsilon F_{p}/2)^{2d}},\hskip 72.26999pt\text{by first part of Lemma ~\ref{lem:dcentmom}.}

\displaystyle\leq(2/5)^{2d}

\displaystyle\leq(2/3)\delta^{2}

since,
d=\lceil\log(1/\delta)\rceil
and
d\geq 1
.

Now, unconditioning with respect to
\mathcal{H}
, we have,
